# Terra Firma — Revival Tracker

Working list for finishing the mod. Update as things land.

**Resolved history lives in TODO-archive.md** (split 2026-08-06; major trim pass 2026-08-11): build records, verify-session logs, superseded specs, decomp ground truth, harness rounds. **Held content lives in TODO-quarantine.md** (created 2026-08-11): the v1 fissure rock catalog, cooking effects, Lithovore — out of design conversations until explicitly reopened. RULED markers in both still count as provenance - grep all three files.

**TEXT + PUNCTUATION HARD RULES (hardened 2026-08-15 at the user's direction, "i feel like we should harden that ruling"). Two rules that look alike and are not the same thing:**

1. **NO EM DASHES IN SHIPPED TEXT** — anything a player reads: `manifest.json` description, blueprint `Description`/`DisplayName`, ability and level text, popups, message-queue lines. Standing since the manifest description sitting; it had lived only in session-prompt docs and one buried manifest note, which is why it kept needing re-stating. **Scope answered 2026-08-15 (user asked): it was NEVER a code rule.** Comments, commit messages and tracker prose are free — and stay free, because the strip pass deletes comments from published builds anyway.
2. **NO `--` ANYWHERE INSIDE AN XML COMMENT** — not style, *syntax*: XML forbids it outright and the file fails to parse. This is the curse that has now struck **TEN times** (twice in the 2026-08-15 sitting alone, both times reaching for an em-dash-flavored aside and typing `--`). Write "i.e.", a semicolon, a comma, or a real — character instead.

**Enforcement, so neither rule depends on memory again:** `tools\check-text-rules.ps1` fails closed on both, scanning every mod XML, .cs and the manifest. It runs inside `publish-beta.ps1` before anything is zipped or staged, and should be run before any deploy. Strict XML validation already catches rule 2 at parse time; the checker catches it *and* names the file and line, and catches rule 1 which nothing was checking at all.

**Tags:** `[bug]` broken behavior · `[polish]` text/UX cleanup · `[design]` needs a creative/balance decision · `[verify]` needs in-game confirmation · `[stretch]` stretch goal

**How we work (2026-07-21):** each mutation gets **one audit pass** — knock out its small fixes, settle its design questions, restyle its description text (base-game voice, see style notes under Gorgon's Gaze), then mark it ready to verify. In-game verification happens in batches, not per-fix. ~~Fissure is vetoed until every other mutation's audit is complete.~~ **Veto condition met; lifted 2026-07-22 — Fissure is the active workstream.**

**Provenance discipline (2026-07-25, after the "gear-warfare doesn't cook" creep — a Claude
proposal hardened into a cited "rule" through nothing but repetition):** every design-shaping
statement in the trackers carries a status tag — **RULED** (date + the user's words) · **LEAN**
(user's, explicitly not a ruling) · **CLAUDE PROPOSAL, UNRULED**. Citing a rule as precedent
requires finding its RULED marker; no marker = it's a proposal and gets cited as one. Scope:
design claims only — delegated code/plumbing decisions need no ratification ceremony.

- [ ] `[bug?]` **LIVE INCIDENT 2026-07-24 (verify-boot 15:42, character "Karuukat" Mutated Human Stonecarver + Calcify + Boar Vessel): per-tick NRE storm, cause UNATTRIBUTED.** Signature: `ERROR Registry.Add Index: -1` twice DURING Joppa zone build (base objects: Wooden Water Wheel/LiquidCovered, WatervineFarmerJoppa/Body), then `RunSegment (final): NullReferenceException at GameObject.TurnTick [0x0003e]` EVERY tick thereafter (27k+ in 20 min, log 12+ MB). Decomp (2.0.211.50): TurnTick walks the registered-handler array — a null Handler entry in a TurnTick registry, i.e. event-registry corruption from build time. Evidence FOR vanilla: zero mod frames in any stack; same class as verification session 1's "vanilla-only event-pool overflow in pit generation" scare; the failing registrations are pure base objects. Evidence AGAINST: first-ever occurrence is the first boot after the instrumentation deploy (prev boot 01:28 same day, same game version, same mod enabled, played clean with 191 TF-VERIFY lines) — though the four instrumented files (Lithovore eat channels, StoneMill menus, TimedForm, Stoneshaper cast) have no per-tick paths and none of their content exists in a fresh Joppa. Deployed folder hash-verified identical to repo; compile clean (37 files). Key fact: the embark auto-save COMPLETED before the first NRE — the on-disk save likely predates the corruption. **RESOLVED-AS-VANILLA 2026-07-24 (same day): relaunch + reload of the same save came back CLEAN** — zero Registry.Add errors, zero NREs, save loaded in 970ms, same mod build byte-for-byte. Verdict: a nondeterministic build-time event-registry race in the base game (second sighting of the class, after verification session 1's pit-generation scare). The instrumentation deploy is cleared — same code produced the corruption-free boot. Downgraded to a WATCH ITEM: if a third sighting lands, capture the log and consider reporting upstream to Freehold. The one MODERROR each boot is the disabled workshop mod "Bit Helper"'s broken manifest JSON — scan-time noise, not ours, ignorable.

- [x] `[bug]` `[design]` **Whorl landing-beat dust poofs + ricochet animation redesign — USER LIVE FLAGS 2026-08-14; FULLY CLOSED GREEN 2026-08-15 (both eyes-signoffs in: rocks "oh it's happening and it's lovely" · lasers "lasers looks fine also" — every beat log-green across three boots, dust gone, sketch-bounce and simultaneous bent beam both confirmed on screen).** Full build/diagnosis record below; open residual = only the repeat-cell juice-skip watch note. (1) **Dust poofs on BOTH swat and rico landings (user: "i'm seeing dust poofs on both of them and i don't want it on either"):** mod-side landing beats are grep-verified grit-only (DustPuff died at the 08-12 correction), so the poofs are NOT ours — prime suspect is the ENGINE's own thrown-landing presentation, which runs when PerformThrow finishes and re-places the item (the `[GVW] deflect mark` lines show the engine completing its own landing at ITS cell before our restore moves the item — the poof may even be firing at the engine's wrong cell). Recon owed at build: decomp PerformThrow's landing FX; natural suppression seam = `Cleo_TerraFirma_WhorlDeflectedPart` already rides the projectile through exactly that window. (2) **Ricochet redesign RULED (user's own words): laser bolts — "i want to redraw the animation of the laser line when it ricochets"; rocks — "i want it to 'bounce' on us and then do a thrown animation in the direction it's ricocheting."** Both replace the staggered gravel-blip trace as the carom's travel language. **Elbow RULED RETIRED same day (user: "bounce redraw carries the whole beat imo") — the chip-burst goes with the trace; bounce (rocks) / beam redraw (lasers) is the entire rico presentation.** Build recon notes, Claude: rock leg = the engine's own thrown-flight arc animation pointed at the carom endpoint (bounce beat at the defender first — small hop, the mod owns the timing); laser leg = a second beam segment along the carom direction — the spark-jack recon found `laser_renderer`/`vls_renderer` are PUBLIC MonoBehaviours with reachable config, so drawing our own segment may be Harmony-free (verify at build; if it needs a Harmony detour into the missile-VFX assembly, that collides with the standing de-Harmony direction and comes back as a menu). Mechanics untouched — carom targeting/damage/landing all stay; this is presentation only. **BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (after-session batch; compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified — active next restart).** Decomp recon that shaped it (all verified in `GameObject.PerformThrow` + `CombatJuice.Throw` + `ProjectileRenderer`): the engine's thrown-flight juice is a missile-VFX config whose `ProjectileRenderer` runs a ParticleSystem ONLY when the config carries `ParticleRate` — the poof is the stone's own `ThrowVFX` xTag dust cluster lingering at the engine's endpoint — and `ConfigureMissileVisualEffectEvent` dispatches to the PROJECTILE'S parts after all defaults load, so the deflect mark's overrides win the config dict. **Zero Harmony anywhere.** The build: `Part_WhorlDeflected` gains `Bounce`/`Ricochet` fields + a `ConfigureMissileVisualEffectEvent` handler (strips `ParticleRate`, re-paths leg 1 to END at the whorl, computes leg-1 flight time the renderer's own way and hands the baton); new `Cleo_TerraFirma_JuiceStoneBounce` juice entry (weapon's own tile on the pooled-sprite lifecycle — hidden through leg 1, vertical hop at the whorl, then a thrown-arc leg to the carom end in the engine's flight grammar 350px/s + spin; rotation ALSO pool-restored at finish); `PlayBeamRedraw` draws the laser carom leg with the bolt's OWN beam prefab (`ProjectileVFX` + `ProjectileVFXConfiguration` lifted off the projectile, `setPathProjectileVFX` — MissileWeapon's exact wiring grammar; light's two legs overlap deliberately, instant light reads as one bent path); elbow + blip trace deleted from tiles mode (trace SURVIVES in classic display mode where sprite flights can't render — one carom language per display mode); swat keeps engine flight (re-pathed to the whorl, dust stripped) + grit loot-pointer; carom-landing grit now skips light bolts (light leaves no grit). `tfwhorlfx` sampler's elbow beat swapped for a bounce+flight demo (3E flying to 6E). CLAUDE dials flagged for feel: BOUNCE_SEC 0.16 / HOP_PX 9 / ARC_PX 6 / spin 240-480°/s. **FLIGHT-INVISIBLE BUG — USER CATCH 2026-08-15 ("i'm not seeing the second leg on the small stones sent careening"), DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED same sitting (compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified — active next restart):** the thrown-leg branch assigned `p.z = Z_CHUNK` as an ABSOLUTE while the house idiom is RELATIVE — and decomp proves the tile plane sits at z=100 (`getTileCenter` returns `100 - z`), so the flight sprite drew 111 units off-plane and NEVER RENDERED ONCE in either build. Explains the whole feel-round arc retroactively: the original hop used the relative form and was the only visible piece — the user's "extra bounce on the player's tile" was the hop rendering while the flight silently wasn't. Fix: `p.z = Bounce.z + Z_CHUNK` (= 89, just in front of the plane, the fleck idiom). Caster-only exemption stands.** Root cause decomp-verified: the engine's base PV of 4 is `RuleSettings.VISUAL_PENETRATION_BONUS` — DISPLAY-ONLY, appended in weapon name-lines and mutation stat text, never rolled (`MeleeWeapon.GetNormalPenetration` = PenBonus 0 + capped Str mod; Stinger/LightManipulation add the 4 only in text). Our ruled "PV 4 + Str, the melee-weapon grammar" fed display units to `RollDamagePenetrations`: at Str mod 3 the wave rolled +7 — a weapon reading ~↔11 — and +7 vs AV 2 cannot fail a pen die. Fix: `GetCreaturePV()` = Strength mod ALONE (true melee grammar; any future PV text adds the visual 4 back). Wall carve PV (6+3L flat) knowingly LEFT AS-IS: tuned as its own dial with verified outcomes, not claiming melee grammar. EB clean (rides engine weapon parts). Level-scaled bonus stays rejected; MaxStrengthBonus-style cap stays reserved. `[verify]` next boot: [FIS] hit lines show pens varying (0-pens now possible vs armored), chaff no longer guaranteed-erased by pulse 1.

## Gorgon's Gaze  ← audit in progress, furthest along

*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md: Done / The big one)*

### Open bugs / verifications
- [x] `[bug]` **MAJOR: gazing a wall = endless-pass LOCKOUT — DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-08-07 (user report from live play, "gorgon's gaze a holographic shale wall ... endless cycle of passing, not a hard freeze"; compiles green, hash-verified, in tfbatch).** Root cause chain (decomp-verified): the player-path picker's `GetCombatTarget(ParentObject, true)` rides the engine default **`AllowInanimate=true`**, so ANY plain wall or furniture is a legal gaze victim — the holographic part was incidental (its invulnerability never even engages). A brainless Target then NREs `TickGaze`'s `Target.Brain.Attacked` on EVERY BeginTakeActionEvent; the engine aborts the player's action each segment, and the gaze can never break because the break rides UseEnergyEvent — an aborted action never spends energy. Same signature class as the 07-24 TurnTick NRE storm: game live, player locked. **The user's animated-wall theory checked and inverted: an ANIMATED wall is a full creature (Brain + donor stat block, and AnimateEvent strips HologramInvulnerability) — it duels and resolves fine; only PLAIN inanimates lock.** Fix, two layers: (1) command gate — brainless victims refused before the energy/cooldown spend, both player path (ShowFail "You cannot transfix {it}." — DRAFT) and AI path (silent invalid); every true creature in Qud carries a Brain, mindless included, so nothing legit is excluded; (2) TickGaze belt — a brainless Target releases silently instead of NRE-ing, which also **SELF-HEALS any save made inside the lockout** (first tick after load releases the gaze). Harness: `feedback.gazewall` drives BeginGaze→TickGaze against a spawned HolographicShaleWall and asserts release-not-throw. `[verify]` via **`tfgazewall`** (user test design, one-wish staging: plain + animated holo wall side by side) — **LIVE RUN 2026-08-07 morning boot, log-confirmed: the ANIMATED wall duel ran end-to-end** (saves rolled with 2 interleaved successes, DC escalated 12→21 per the +1/fail law, band FX tracked net 0→7, 10th cumulative fail → PETRIFIED, statue + entombed possessions, zero exceptions in the log) **and `feedback.gazewall` PASS same boot** (brainless release belt fired, no NRE). **PLAIN wall refusal CONFIRMED same boot (user: "the holoshale wall refused me so that's good") — the gaze-wall fix is FULLY GREEN:** gate ✓ duel ✓ belt ✓. Residual (only if a locked save ever surfaces): the self-heal ride is code-identical to the harness belt, expected to just work.
- [ ] `[bug?]` `[verify]` **Dead-gorgon trap — DEFERRED FROM THE VERIFY PASS (user ruling 2026-07-24: "no NPCs have gorgon's gaze, so we can flag those for not needing verification right now"):** the fix (effect self-recedes when `Initiator` invalid, own EndTurnEvent) is BUILT and instrumented (`GAZE DEAD-GORGON RELEASE` line); it just cannot fire until an NPC gorgon exists to kill mid-channel. Verify lands with the creatures/quarriers workstream (same box as the parked LOS-break dial). Original diagnosis kept: if the caster dies mid-gaze, `TickGaze` stops firing and the `Petrifying` effect doesn't count down while active.
- [ ] `[verify]` **Stunned/confused/asleep caster — DEFERRED FROM THE VERIFY PASS (same 2026-07-24 ruling, same gate):** needs an NPC gorgon to stun. Sunder Mind explicitly breaks its channel on such effects; Gorgon's has no equivalent check — test when NPC gorgons exist; add an explicit check if a stunned gorgon keeps gazing.

### 0.1.2 board answers (user question 2026-08-13, answered from code — ground truth, not memory)
- **Statue inventory = EXACT COPY, never a re-roll.** `_StatueHelpers.EntombPossessions` (shared by Gaze petrify-kills and Stoneshaper expiry swaps): statue becomes a real container (Chest recipe, Container+Inventory), every non-natural equipped item is unequipped, then the LITERAL GameObject instances move from victim inventory into the statue — stacks, mods, liquid fills, everything as-held at petrify moment (spawn-time table roll is the only roll that ever happened). Natural equipment stays with the body by design; `ReturnPossessions` unwinds it all if the death is prevented.
- [ ] `[bug]` **EQUIPPED GEAR IS DESTROYED BY PETRIFICATION, NOT ENTOMBED — USER REPORT 2026-08-15, live ("it makes sure that you can get their inventory, but it might not force unequip all their items before freezing them… no butchery is fine, but armor should be lootable"). DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED same sitting (user: "let's do our one line fix for statues!"; compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified, active next restart).** The user's read was right and the damage is one step worse than "not lootable": the gear is **orphaned**, not merely left behind. Chain, all verified in `BodyPart.cs`: `_StatueHelpers.EntombPossessions` loops the body and calls **`part.Unequip()`**, which is the LOW-LEVEL SLOT CLEARER — its whole body is `FindPartsEquipping(equipped, …)` then `ClearEquipped()` on each. It fires no unequip command and hands the item to no inventory. Equipped objects do not live in `Inventory` (they live on the body part), so after that call the armor is referenced by nothing, and the sweep that follows moves only what was already in the pack. Hence exactly the reported symptom: statue opens, backpack is there, everything WORN or WIELDED is gone. **Scope is wider than the gaze:** the same helper runs on Stoneshaper's expiry swap (`Mutation_Stoneshaper.cs:720`), so statue-swap loot has the identical hole. **Fix shape (one line, not built): `part.ForceUnequip(Silent: true)`** — fires `CommandForceUnequipObject`, the engine's own forced path, which routes the item into the owner's inventory and handles multi-slot and cursed gear correctly; the existing inventory sweep then carries it to the statue unchanged. Considered alternative: capture `part.Equipped` and `Statue.ReceiveObject` it directly, fewer engine assumptions but it skips the unequip event that some items key off — ForceUnequip preferred. **RECORD CORRECTION, same sitting: the 08-13 board answer below claims "every non-natural equipped item is unequipped, then the LITERAL GameObject instances move from victim inventory into the statue." The first half is true, the second half never applied to equipped gear at all.** User's scope confirmed at report: corpse-path loss (butchery, pelts) stays as ruled, this is only about carried and worn goods. **BUILT AS DIAGNOSED:** `ForceUnequip(Silent: true)` replaces the slot-clear, with two hardenings the one-liner did not have — the parts list is SNAPSHOTTED before the loop (the unequip event can restructure the body underneath a live enumeration, which the old code was also exposed to) and a BELT catches a refusal: if `ForceUnequip` returns false and the item is still equipped, the slot is cleared the old way *and the item handed straight to the statue in the same breath*, so nothing can be orphaned again by any path. **Harness probe added, `feedback.entomb` (rides `tffeedback`/`tfbatch`):** equips armor + carries boots on a test Snapjaw, entombs, asserts BOTH land inside the statue; it distinguishes the three failure modes in its own report line (setup fail / worn lost / carried lost). **tfbatch expectation moves 24 → 25.** `[verify]` next boot: `tfbatch` green including `feedback.entomb`, then eyes on a real gaze kill — statue holds the victim's armor and weapon, not just the pack. **Knock-on flagged, not built:** `ReturnPossessions` (death-prevented unwind) hands everything back to INVENTORY and does not re-equip. Before the fix that path returned nothing at all for worn gear, so this is strictly an improvement, but a survivor now wakes up holding their armor rather than wearing it — auto-re-equip on return is a separate call, user's.
- **Chosen-not-discovered rule (Claude observation at the trace, ENDORSED lean, user veto open): petrify kills produce NO corpse, so corpse-path loot never rolls — gaze forecloses butchery.** A gaze-killed cobble baboon entombs his carried stones but never yields cobblefur; the rockhound's swallowed hoard DOES entomb (it's inventory), but pelts/butcherables/the stonefrog's ruled rock-corpse are corpse-path and lost to stone. Flavor read: "you can't skin stone." Recorded so the rule is deliberate rather than stumbled into at a bug report.

## Earthen Barrage

*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md: Small fixes)*

- [x] **Feedback-thread item CHECKED, ALREADY IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-07, user: "earthen barrage should visually become larger stones as you level ... ignore me if it already does"):** it does — pebble/stone/boulder at Lv1/5/10 across name (`GetStoneName`), sprite (`GetStoneCosmeticData`: sw_smallstone → sw_stone_large → sw_mediumboulder), AND throw/impact sfx weight. The reporter has likely only seen sub-Lv5 pebbles. Known non-issue: a held stone doesn't re-dress at the threshold moment, but every throw/re-arm sculpts fresh and held stones ride a short lease — stale window is a few turns, no code warranted.
- [x] `[bug]` **SUBSCRIBER REPORT 2026-08-12 (relayed near-verbatim by the user): "Take Stones (x/y)" ambiguity + "needing to toggle" after pickup — DIAGNOSED + BOTH FIXES BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (compiles green 0 errors, Objects.xml strict-valid, hash-verified — active next restart).** The subscriber reverse-engineered the state machine themselves and was right on both counts: the counter reads the RESERVE only (equipped stone lives outside it), so "0/3" meant both armed-with-empty-reserve and truly dry; and the pickup path missed every ruled re-arm trigger. Pickup mechanism: a stone taken off the ground lands in inventory, the end-of-turn reabsorb (07-23 "held intention" ruling) refunds the charge but never re-arms, AND the refund poisons the regen backstop (its ran-dry QoL literally checks `Charges == 1` right after its own increment — a refund-to-1 makes the next tick land on 2 and the check never fires; stuck unarmed until a manual toggle cycle). **Fixes, both RULED at the 2026-08-12 sitting: (1) display = "(x/y+1)" — USER'S OWN FORMAT ("0/3+1 makes sense as like 'i have no stones in reserve and 1 equipped'"), "+1" suffix while a sculpted stone rides the thrown slot, synced on throw/EndTurn so it can't go stale. (2) pickup re-arm via TakenEvent, the thorn-dodging shape (user raised the can't-unequip worry; converged on event-split): new `Scripts/Part_StoneRearmOnTake.cs` (`Cleo_TerraFirma_StoneRearmOnTakePart`) on the EphemeralStone blueprint (resonant inherits) pokes `OnStoneTaken` — decomp-verified that TakenEvent fires ONLY on the take-from-world flow (sculpts arrive via ReceiveObject→AddedToInventoryEvent), so picking up one of our own stones with toggle on + empty slot equips it straight back (lease refreshed), while a DELIBERATE unequip-into-pack fires no TakenEvent and keeps the 07-23 reabsorb ruling untouched.** Scope guards, recorded: plain rocks (IsAnyStone) deliberately excluded — auto-wielding ordinary loot would hijack scavenging; a dry thrower still self-heals on the regen tick. The poisoned `Charges == 1` backstop LEFT AS-RULED: with pickup handled at the event, no realistic path reaches reabsorb-while-dry-and-unarmed (sculpted stones crumble in 50-100r; nobody trades them). Dials flagged, unruled: pickup-equip is SILENT (the take message already printed; a "you ready the stone" line is available if wanted) · an attuned thrower picking up a NORMAL stone equips it as-is, no auto-retune (free retune via the Attune toggle exists). `[verify]` next boot: throw dry → pick up own stone → it equips + label reads "(0/3+1)" · deliberate unequip into pack still reabsorbs WITHOUT re-equipping · dry throw drops the "+1" · label at full reserve while armed reads "(3/3+1)" (or level equivalent). **SAME-DAY LIVE-CATCH + CORRECTION (user, first live session on the build: "equipping anything in your thrown slot while a sculpted pebble is in your hand fully deletes the stone without adding it to your reserve pouch" — CONFIRMED MINE, FIXED + REDEPLOYED same sitting, compiles green, hash-verified):** the "TakenEvent fires only on take-from-world" premise was WRONG — decomp: the unequip-to-inventory flow ends in `eCommandFreeTakeObject`, which IS a `CommandTakeObject` (EnergyCost 0), so displaced equipment also arrives via PerformTake/TakenEvent. The ungated part re-equipped the stone into the slot the engine was mid-clearing on every swap; the stone died in the fight (log signature: ZERO reabsorb lines all session — it never rested in inventory). Fix: the part snapshots `BeginBeingTaken` (fires on the item BEFORE it leaves its old context) — in-a-cell = true ground pickup, acts; equipped/container = shuffle, stays out, the 07-23 reabsorb governs. Positive side-effect of the gate: container takes don't auto-equip either (rummaging ≠ stooping). Ground-pickup re-arm was CONFIRMED WORKING live before the correction (multiple `[EB] pickup re-arm` log lines, user's own session) and the gate does not touch that path. `[verify]` re-run: swap another thrown weapon over a held stone → stone lands in pack and reabsorbs at end of turn (refund below cap / crumble at cap, message either way) · manual unequip-to-pack same · ground pickup still re-arms.
- [ ] `[design]` **Salvage-not-reset lease on picked-up stones — USER LEAN 2026-08-12, recorded not scheduled ("i definitely think picked up stones keep their original cooldown could be fun, but i'm not necessarily strictly wanting to implement it right now"):** currently a pickup re-arm puts the stone back on the held lease and the next throw re-rolls a fresh 50-100r crumble window, so a recycled stone's lifetime is per-excursion. The dial: picked-up stones keep their original countdown instead (one-line change in `OnStoneTaken` — skip the lease refresh, let the ground clock ride). Windfall audit from the same sitting (why nothing is urgent): recovery costs combat action economy, restores offense but not the whorl/reserve defense, stones are untradeable ephemera, and scav baboons already live the loop. Pick up at a feel pass if dry-thrower recycling ever reads too generous.

### Design calls
- [ ] `[design]` **Daze rider CUT — RULED 2026-08-06 (beta rebalance pass, item 3; user: "cutting the daze rider from earthen barrage") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** Constants, `GetThrowDazeChanceIn100`, the `MakeStone` part attachment, the level-text line, the CollectStats stat, and the ability-card clause all removed. `Part_DazeOnThrowPens.cs` kept as an ORPHAN on purpose — earmarked for a wild rock-thrower variant (cragmensch note, TODO-archive benchmarks). Companion proposals from the same item (whorl stat gains, damage curve) tracked separately below, UNRULED at time of writing. `[verify]` thrown stones no longer daze; level text and ability card show no daze line.
- [ ] `[design]` **Whorl stat shifts CUT — RULED 2026-08-06 (user: "cut stat shift from whorl") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** The reserve-size AV (+1 at 3+, +2 at 9+) and the resonant DV swap (+2/+4, the 2026-07-23 compensation ruling — superseded with its parent) are gone; `Sync` now clears any lingering shift (old-save hygiene) and the whorl's defense is deflection alone (3%/stone +1%/level, outcomes 50/30/20). Level-text AV lines and the ability-card "grants DV in place of AV" clause removed; the parked whorl-DV display fix is MOOT. `[verify]` reserve at 9+: character sheet shows no whorl AV/DV; attune on/off flips deflect type only.
- [ ] `[design]` **Throw damage curve — PEN CUT RULED 2026-08-06 (user data point: "one shotting electrofuges at level 4"; from the simmed menu: "I lean penetration cut, yeag") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** Pen bonus Lv/2 → **Lv/3** (+1/+2/+3 at 3/6/9); damage dice UNTOUCHED at (1+L/3)d6+(L%3). Sim ground truth (`design/eb_damage_sim.py` + electrofuge one-shot runs, Toughness HP modifier included per user reminder — Leveler.cs applies TouMod×(Lv−1)): P(one-shot electrofuge | hit) at L4 drops 19-37% → 8-21%; L10 avg vs AV 3 drops 44 → 29, landing at Light Manipulation lase parity (28) — defensible as "EB per-hit ≈ lase, paying the to-hit/range tax with a bigger magazine." Dice-cut and both-cut variants REJECTED (both-cut undershot lase). Rider supersession: resonant pens (trained/5, "+1 at Lv10" per the 2026-07-22 ruling) is now 0 at every level — resonant stones ride pure vibro; noted in MakeStone. Every-level lattice intact (damage bonus/dice tick all ten levels). `[verify]` L4 stones read 2d6+1 pv+1 on the sheet; electrofuges survive most single hits at L4; resonant stones show no pen bonus.
- [ ] `[design]` **Damage still reads too high — RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-15 (ladder F; opened by the user note "note: tune earthen barrage damage lower").** Successor to the pen cut above: that pass took penetration only and left the dice untouched at (1+L/3)d6+(L%3), on the reasoning that L10 avg vs AV 3 landed at Light Manipulation lase parity (29 vs 28). This note says the result still hits harder than wanted — which puts the dice back on the table, since pen is the half already spent. The dice-cut variant REJECTED on 2026-08-06 is the obvious first thing to re-sim, but its rejection was relative to lase parity, so settle the target first: is parity-with-lase still the yardstick, or does EB now aim below it? Re-run `design/eb_damage_sim.py` (Toughness HP modifier on) once the target is set; the electrofuge one-shot runs are the comparable. **Open question for the user: which lever, and what number does it aim at.**
  - **Friend's lever menu, relayed by the user 2026-08-15 (verbatim):** *"i think its fun that u can use it a lot so maybe just less damage to start? / or maybe it Should just come back slower / or fewer in the chamber? / or have a decent amount of weight / but then that makes it even worse lategame when it probably falls off quite a bit anyway"* — four levers, thinking out loud, nothing ruled. Claude's read (PROPOSAL, unruled), with the code checked at time of writing: **(a) less damage to start = the live one.** It is the only lever pointed at the actual complaint (`GetThrowDamage` = `(1+L/3)d6+(L%3)`, L4 = 2d6+1, the electrofuge one-shot level) and it is the only one that respects the friend's own stated value, that using it a lot is the fun part. **(b) slower regen — misses.** `TURNS_PER_CHARGE = 15`, Willpower-scaled; slowing it changes how OFTEN you one-shot an electrofuge, never WHETHER you do, and it flattens the mod's second Willpower payoff (the "earth rewards the disciplined" theme it shares with Gorgon's Gaze DC). **(c) fewer in the chamber — double-dip trap, recommend against.** Reserve size IS the whorl: `GetDeflectChance() = (3 * Charges) + Level`, and `MaxCharges = 3 + (Lv-1)` runs 3 at L1 to 12 at L10, so cutting the magazine nerfs offense and defense on one dial. The whorl already paid at the 2026-08-06 stat-shift cut. **(d) weight — near-dead already.** The sculpted stone ships `Weight="1"` in `Objects.xml` and engine weight is an int, so the only moves up are whole points carried per stone in reserve, worst exactly at L10 with 12 of them. The friend talked themself out of this one in the same breath and they were right. *(Do not confuse this with the 2026-07-24 "pebble weight 0" ratification, which was a rock-catalog item, now quarantined.)*
  - **The friend's closing worry is the most useful line in the note, and it cuts against the last pass.** They flag lategame falloff. The 2026-08-06 pen cut (Lv/2 → Lv/3) took **1 pen at L4 and 2 at L10** — it was steeper LATE, i.e. it already pushed hardest where the falloff worry lives. So the shape to sim is not a flat damage cut but an **early-weighted** one: move where the dice ladder ticks (`diceSize` off `L/3`) so L1-6 comes down and L10 lands near where it is now. **Still blocked on the same open question: parity-with-lase as the target, or below it.**
  - **SIM RUN 2026-08-15 (user: "sure, we can sim it") — `design/eb_damage_sim2.py`, results DATA not ruling; no code touched.** Engine validated against the 2026-08-06 record before anything new was read: it reproduces that pass's L10-vs-AV3 averages (pre-cut 44.2 vs recorded 44, post-cut 29.2 vs recorded 29) and its electrofuge one-shot band (pre 19-37%, post 9-21% vs recorded 19-37% / 8-21%) to the digit. **Electrofuge HP band 18-22 was BACK-SOLVED from those recorded percentages, not re-derived** — flagged in the script docstring, because reading `BaseSpider` Toughness (10+1d3 = 11-13, modifier NEGATIVE) off the blueprint suggests effective HP 6-10 instead, which would roughly triple every one-shot number. Unresolvable this session, no decomp on disk. It moves all rows together and does not reorder the candidates, so the ranking below holds either way; settle it before any number ships.
    - **The complaint is a CEILING problem, not an average problem** — this is the finding that picks the lever. Damage is rolled once *per penetration*, so the dice count multiplies. At L4 shipped (2d6+1) a two-pen throw tops out at 26; the electrofuge dies at 18-22. Trading dice for flat bonus at the same average pulls the ceiling down without gutting the curve, which is why a **flat average cut is the wrong shape** and the every-level lattice can survive intact.
    - **Ladder F — slow start, L10 identical. RECOMMENDED (Claude proposal, unruled).** `1d6+0 / 1d6+1 / 1d6+2 / 1d6+3 / 2d6+0 / 2d6+1 / 3d6+0 / 3d6+1 / 4d6+0 / 4d6+1` at L1-10, pen ladder untouched at L/3. L4 one-shot **9-21% → 2-8%**. L7 avg vs AV3 18.0 → 16.4. **L10 avg vs AV3 stays 29.2 — the exact shipped number, still Light Manipulation lase parity (28).** Answers "less damage to start" while touching the lategame falloff not at all. Ticks every level.
    - **Ladder G — F plus a lategame trim.** Same start, one notch off the top: L10 = `4d6+0`, avg vs AV3 27.2, a hair under lase. Take this one only if the answer to the target question is "below parity."
    - **Ladder E — the mild version.** Whole ladder shifted one level left (L4 `2d6+0`, L10 `4d6+0`). Cuts everything ~6-7% uniformly: L4 one-shot 4-13%, L10 avg 27.3. Cheapest to implement (two off-by-one edits, no table) but it is the flat-cut shape the ceiling finding argues against.
    - **The 2026-08-06 dice-cut rejection is CONFIRMED, with a new reason.** d4 dice put L10 at **21.4 vs AV3** — not just under lase, but hollowing out exactly the lategame the friend's closing line worries about. It buys the best early number (L4 one-shot 1-5%) at the worst possible price. Stays rejected.
  - **LADDER F RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "we can go with F") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same sitting.** `GetThrowDamage` in `Mutation_EarthenBarrage.cs` is now a hand-authored table for L1-10 (`LADDER_DICE`/`LADDER_FLAT`) with L11+ falling through to the pre-existing `(1+L/3)d6+(L%3)` curve; the two meet exactly at L10 (`4d6+1`), so there is no seam and wish-level scaling is untouched. Shipped ladder: `1d6 / 1d6+1 / 1d6+2 / 1d6+3 / 2d6 / 2d6+1 / 3d6 / 3d6+1 / 4d6 / 4d6+1`. Penetration NOT touched (`GetPenetrationCap` stays `1 + Lv/3`). Compiles green, 0 errors; deployed to `Mods\TerraFirma` and `Mods\TerraFirmaBeta`, both hash-verified; active next game restart. **No text debt:** the level text and `CollectStats` both read `GetThrowDamage(Level)` live, and no dice are hardcoded in any XML, so the ability card and level lines re-derive themselves.

  - `[open]` **Electrofuge effective-HP derivation still unsettled — carried forward from the sim, does NOT block the ladder.** The 18-22 band was back-solved from the 2026-08-06 percentages; the blueprint reading (`BaseSpider` Toughness 10+1d3, negative modifier) argues for 6-10 instead. It scales every one-shot figure together without reordering the candidates, which is why F shipped anyway. While toggled on with stones in reserve: deflect chance vs *physical* projectiles = 3% × current charges + 1% × level (46% at Lv10 full reserve); lasers/plasma/electric pass through (beam attacks bypass inherently — they never fire `DefenderMissileHitEvent`; energy *weapons* filtered by projectile attributes); +1 AV at 3+ stones, +2 at 9+. Deflecting costs nothing — **throwing depletes the shield**; hoard-or-hurl is the tension. Modeled on Calcify's `WhorlingStones` code shape.
- [x] `[bug]` **Resonant penetration double-dip (user playtest find 2026-07-22, RULED −5):** the level-scaled PenetrationBonus (+5 at Lv10) stacked on top of Vorpal's pen=AV matching, rolling ~6 penetrations vs ANY armor — resonant did ~60 to snapjaws vs normal stones' ~30, inverting the designed trade (resonant = flat ~23 vs everything, eb_sim.py). **Fixed in MakeStone (ruling refined twice, final):** attuned stones keep **one fifth** of the level-scaled bonus (`scaledPens / 5` — +1 at Lv10, scales at wish-levels; earlier forms −5-flat and flat-1 superseded). Covers both sculpt and in-place retune (both route through MakeStone). Level text notes the trade. `[verify]` resonant vs snapjaw ≈ 1 penetration ~10 dmg; vs Cragmensch still ÷ AV-matched.

Icon is a cyan-tinted reuse of the takestones tile — swap for bespoke art whenever. Spec (for reference): A second toggle ability (gated ~Lv10, tunable) that attunes your stones: they gain the adjective **{{C|resonant}}** (cyan) and vibro penetration (PV=AV), base form/name ladder unchanged. Sim grounding (`eb_sim.py`): resonant = flat ~23 avg vs any armor — Cragmensch 1 throw, Lithofex burstable, Chrome Pyramid still no (171) — while normal stones do 68 vs AV 3; a true choice, not an upgrade.
  - **Toggling retunes the stone already in hand, in place, at no charge cost** (free action) — never wastes a stone for a player who forgets to pre-toggle; auto-equip continues sculpting whichever mode is active.

Resonant whorl: deflects **Light-tagged projectiles** (laser rifle/pistol fire — verified `Attributes="Light Laser"` in game blueprints; filter = attributes contain `Light`), physical projectiles pass through, and the AV bonus becomes **+2/+4 DV (RULED 2026-07-23, supersedes the provisional +2/+3: "DV is less valuable than AV so you need more to compensate" — the full 1 AV→2 DV rate; effect + level text updated, deployed)**. Same deflect-chance formula both modes. One toggle retunes offense and defense to the same world: mass vs energy — preserves "a choice, not an upgrade." Beam attacks (Lase-type) bypass both modes (engine: no missile event) — say so in the description text.
  - Implementation notes: melee half = `VibroWeapon ChargeUse="0"` (precedent: the mod's statue fist); thrown half = `Vorpal` attribute on `ThrownWeapon` (what the engine's ÷ penetration display keys off) — **verify the thrown path in-game**. Daze rider unchanged in both modes.
  - **Division of labor:** cleanest shape is a second blueprint (`Cleo_TerraFirma_EphemeralStoneResonant` inheriting the base stone: cyan Render colors, VibroWeapon/Vorpal, flavor description) — **user offered to own the XML**; C# side picks the blueprint by toggle state and handles the in-place retune.

### Whorl outcome scale — **BUILT 2026-07-22 (re-ruled same day; supersedes the 2026-07-21 spec below in three places)**
The three-stage deflection landed (`Effect_GravelWhorl.cs`: `Devour`/`Swat`/`Ricochet`, dispatched on a successful DeflectChance roll). Today's rulings vs the old spec:
- **Split RULED: even thirds (1d3)** — supersedes the old 50/30/20. **RE-RULED same day (third round): back to 50/30/20** — user asked which works better; Claude's case: rarity graded to drama (devour common = the mass hungers and feeds the identity; swat mundane middle; ricochet at 20% stays a story instead of noise). Named consequence, accepted: grenade stones now get torn apart on the defender in HALF of deflects rather than a third — the mind-game sharpens.
- **Devoured:** ephemera obliterate; **plain stones (`IsAnyStone`) feed the reserve +1 charge, capped at max** (as originally specced — enemy rock-throwers feed the barrager; "The whorl thickens." DRAFT message; `Charges++` + `SyncWhorl`, plumbing verified public). **DANGEROUS stones don't join the mass — RULED 2026-07-22 (second ruling round): they're TORN APART in the gravelly mass and their effects TRIGGER.** Boundary = the mill's own hazard class (`IGrenade` part OR `GrindSpawn` tag): grenade stones detonate **at the defender's cell** (a quarrelquartz eaten by your own whorl goes off on you — the mind game vs whorl-users is intended; blame attribution = the thrower via `E.Attacker`, so their kill not your suicide), grind-special stones release their contents around the defender (gyrestone → vortices in your lap, the mill's scatter shape). No charge gained either way. **DELTA — RULED KEPT 2026-07-23: non-stone real items are OBLITERATED** (user: "throwable items are almost always disposable… if you were throwing expensive items at an enemy's whorl to see what happens, you deserve to find out"). Rider from the same ruling, BUILT + DEPLOYED: the torn-apart branch (grenades detonate at the defender, blame to the thrower) now applies to **ALL devoured grenades, not just mod stones** — "as long as when a grenade hits the whorl, it explodes"; a base-game grenade is no longer silently defused. `[verify]` if an enemy-thrown base-game grenade can even reach the outcome scale (grenades usually target cells, not creatures).
- **Swatted:** the original behavior verbatim (munitions fizzle; real items to a random open adjacent cell).
- **Ricochet RULED (after checking the Light Manipulation precedent at user request — base reflect is ANGLE-based, `Direction = Angle + 180`, never target-picked): fully random angle line-trace** — from the defender's cell, range 5 (DRAFT), first creature on the line takes flat d20 vs DV (no marksmanship) and **the projectile's own damage** (ThrownWeapon die / Projectile BaseDamage; supersedes the old −2 PV `RollDamagePenetrations` mini-attack — simpler, no pen math); carom ends at the first body hit or flinched; walls stop it; real items land where it ends, munitions spend themselves; kill attribution = whorl's owner; **payload channels deliberately don't fire** (the whorl redirects mass, not triggers). Applies identically to resonant-mode deflects (light munitions careen too).
- [x] `[bug]` **Unequipped sculpted stones pocketed as inventory items — RULED + FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23 (active next restart):** manually unequipping an EB sculpted stone left it sitting in the pack as a possession. User ruling: "return back to the take stones option, or be destroyed" — implemented as both: at end of turn, any sculpted stone in the caster's INVENTORY is reabsorbed (charge refunded when the reserve has room, crumbles when full). Deliberately end-of-turn rather than the unequip event — throws also unequip mid-flight, and an unequip hook would refund stones actually thrown. Message "You return the stone to the earth." DRAFT. `[verify]` unequip a sculpted stone → it vanishes next turn + charge count rises; thrown stones still spend normally.
- [ ] `[design]` **Aegischist thrown = force BUBBLE around the victim, impassable (user ruling flag, 2026-07-23):** hitting an enemy should cage them in a ring of force shards **they cannot move through** — a thrown prison — replacing the current 3-cell wall line on hits (the line doesn't enclose anything; live-fire showed victims just walking away). Build shape: the thrown-hit path reuses the existing step channel's bubble recipe (ring of 8 around the target); keep the wall line for misses (terrain denial at the landing cell). Build-time check: base `Forcefield` pass semantics — ensure the VICTIM can't slip through (owner-pass rules; shards may need to be ownerless or thrower-owned), and decide whether the ring skipping occupied/solid cells leaves exploitable gaps (wall-adjacent enemies get partial cages — acceptable or fill-with-what).
- [x] `[polish]` **Whorl message conjugation typo — FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23 (active next restart):** "your gravel whorl **send** the laser beam careening". Root cause (decomp-confirmed, `Messaging.XDidY`): the calls passed BOTH the possessive string subject AND `Actor: Object` — the engine conjugates via `Actor.GetVerb`, so a player-owned whorl got second-person verbs ("send") under a third-person subject; NPC whorls read correctly by coincidence. Fix, all nine call sites / five verbs in `Effect_GravelWhorl.cs`: verbs pre-conjugated third-person ("devours"/"tears"/"deflects"/"swats"/"sends"), `Actor: Object` → `Source: Object` (Source keeps the visibility gate `UseVisibilityOf ?? Source ?? Actor` and message routing; a null-Actor with no Source would have silently dropped the messages). `[verify]` each outcome message reads correctly in-game.
- [x] `[bug?]` **Whorl-deflected THROWN items: placement drift + fragility — RULED (b) + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23.** (The find: `PerformThrow` finishes the throw AFTER Devour/Swat/Ricochet acts — re-places the weapon at its own landing cell, overriding the outcome's placement, and the deflected rock then rolled throw-fragility and broke, undercutting "loot preserved.") Ruling: mark the projectile. New transient part `Cleo_TerraFirma_WhorlDeflectedPart` — Swat/Ricochet attach it to real thrown weapons with the outcome's landing cell; at `AfterThrownEvent` it restores that placement (if the engine moved it) and removes itself; `FallenRockPart` calls its `Restore()` FIRST (so miss-payloads/deferred pulses fire from the right cell) and skips the fragility roll while marked (a deflection is the whorl's use, not the thrower's). Added at runtime = always last in the part list, so self-removal mid-dispatch is safe. `[verify]` deflected thrown rock rests where the message says + survives (no fragility break); non-rock thrown items (javelin) also rest at the outcome's cell.
- [ ] `[verify]` outcome scale: deflects split visibly three ways over a fight; devoured plain stone → charge count ticks up (capped) + message; devoured quarrelquartz → detonates ON the defender (and the death message blames the thrower); devoured gyrestone → vortices around the defender; devoured arrow simply gone; ricochet line now renders a staggered gravel-blip trace + endpoint dust puff (RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23; "we could mess around with animations later on" — richer VFX parked as a future pass) — verify the trace reads as travel and hits/flinches/landings resolve, including friendly fire and return-to-sender geometry; whorl's effect description shows the new outcome text; resonant whorl still only touches Light projectiles.
- **Resonant `RefractLight` upgrade — BUILT 2026-07-22** (the 2026-07-21 decision, closed): the effect registers the `"RefractLight"` string event (BlinkingTicSickness idiom: `RegisterEffectEvent` in Apply, unregister in Remove — registered always, handler gates on Resonant since Sync flips attunement at runtime); on success (same DeflectChance) it stamps By + Verb "scatter" (DRAFT) and returns false. **Resonant whorls now scatter Lase-type beams** — the "direct beams pass through" gap closes for resonant mode (normal mode keeps it; effect description texts updated both branches, DRAFT). Two decompile findings shaped it: **(1) beams-only gating** — laser missile SHOTS fire the same event with a `Projectile` param set (MissileWeapon.cs:1426) and are excluded by the handler, because they already roll the missile-hit outcome scale (no double-dip; without the gate resonant would get two bites at every laser bolt, ~1−(1−c)²); Lase beams pass `Projectile=null` (LightManipulation.cs:159). **(2) Fully random scatter is engine-native** — both beam paths pre-seed `Direction = Stat.Random(0,359)` and the handler deliberately does NOT override it (the old spec's `RetroVariance`-style angle math turned out unnecessary — an unset Direction IS the gravel scatter). Sound = engine default `sfx_light_refract`.
- [ ] `[verify]` beam-scatter: resonant whorl + hostile Lase = beams sometimes scatter off you in random directions with the refract sound and "X scatters the laser beam!"; scattered beams can hit bystanders (engine walks the new path); normal-mode whorl never scatters beams; laser PISTOL shots still resolve through the outcome scale only (watch for double messages — the no-double-dip gate); scatter survives save/load (string-event registration across saves — the one serialization unknown).

<details><summary>Superseded 2026-07-21 spec (reference)</summary>
Eaten ~50% (real items drop at feet; stones absorb +1), Deflected ~30%, Ricochet ~20% or 10%+1%/Lv (2–4 cells, −2 PV mini-attack via RollDamagePenetrations). Physical only — the engine has no refract system for non-light projectiles (verified 2026-07-21).

- [ ] `[polish]` **"Rough" statue look-text claims "intricately depicts" (Discord-feedback thread 2026-08-07, a friend's catch relayed by the user; user's "might be fixed now" hunch checked — NOT fixed, the 08-06 true-copy pass only touched WALL descriptions):** "intricate" appears nowhere in our files or git history — it's hard-coded in the base `RandomStatue.SetCreature` template ("This sculpture worked from {material} intricately depicts {creature}: ..."), stamped on every carve at `MakeStatueOfCreatureFromWall`'s `SetCreature(Muse)` call. Scope = Stoneshaper carves only (Gorgon statues never enter this path; sole `SetCreature` caller checked). DeanimateStatue copies `_Short` to the inert statue, so a carve-time fix rides through expiry. **RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-07 (compiles green, hash-verified — active next restart): TIER-1-ONLY fix** — after the six-tier lookbox mockup sitting (full draft ladder rendered: crudely/finely/faithfully/intricately-rehomed/masterfully/flawlessly), the user chose the minimal cut ("we could go even simpler and *just* do tier 1"; Claude concurred — rough is the only tier that CONTRADICTS the base sentence, the others merely restate the name's tier signal). Rough carves now read **"crudely depicts" (user's own word, from "rough statues depicted crudely" — no draft ceremony)** via a `NamePrefix == "rough"` string replace after `SetCreature` in `MakeStatueOfCreatureFromWall`; every other tier keeps the untouched base template (maximal base-voice adherence). Name ladder untouched — display names still climb rough→immaculate. The descriptor switch is the standing hook if the full ladder ever seems worth it (draft words above are the bench). Rides into the inert statue via DeanimateStatue's `_Short` copy. `[verify]` next boot: Lv1-2 carve look-text reads "crudely depicts"; a Lv3+ carve still reads "intricately depicts"; expired rough statue keeps "crudely".
- [x] `[design]` **Distraction fossil → tank statue — RULED 2026-07-21: revive as taunt. BUILT** (uncommented, `Statue.Brain.Passive` removed). Residual watch item (polish pass): user note "hopefully not too strong" — watch in testing; immunity mechanic should self-balance.
  *(superseded text below kept for reference)* Original proposal: User sees value in a statue that draws aggro — unique among familiars. Verified 2026-07-21: the commented block still compiles against the current `Distraction` part; revival = uncomment + delete `Statue.Brain.Passive = true;`. Mechanic niceties (decompiled): a successful save grants `DistractionImmunity` for 3600–8400 turns (taunt checks each enemy once — no per-turn spam, strong-willed enemies opt out cleanly), and it never affects the player (future wild stoneshapers can't taunt-lock you). Save is Willpower vs 10+Lv — another Willpower-theme touchpoint, on the enemy's side. Options: (a) delete fossil; **(c) revive as taunt — recommended**; statue stats (25 HP/Lv, wall AV, modest damage) already profile as a tank.
- [ ] `[design]` `[verify]` Ensure it feels balanced and actually copies the creature's anatomy (`Body.Rebuild(Creature.Body.Anatomy)` — API verified real; test in-game against weird body plans: Slog, swarms, no-handed creatures).
- [ ] `[design]` **Statue-of-a-statue recursion — USER CONCEPT 2026-08-12 (friend report relayed: "with base cooldown, you can make a rough stone sculpture of a rough stone sculpture of a creature"; user's shape, their words: "rather than bar off this behavior entirely, what if we just gave it a random stat malus, so it was like 'each copy of a copy you make degrades'"). TIMING RULED same sitting (user: "i don't think it should happen the *first* time you make a copy, but every time you copy the statue after that, the stats should get dinged") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified — active next restart).** Recon that shaped it (code, verified): a living statue passes the muse picker's `IsCreature`+`Body` gates, so the loop is real; the actual stakes are STATLINE IMMORTALITY — `ApplyStatueBuffs` copies Str/Agi/Tou from the muse, so re-carving your statue from itself before expiry preserves a dead/departed muse's statline forever, dodging the recorded temporary-by-design intent (Lv1-9). HP/AV are formula-driven and don't ride the loop. Build: every statue gets int property `Cleo_TerraFirma_StatueCopyGen` = muse's gen + 1 (real creatures read 0 → their statue is gen 1; the counter exists ONLY for the first-copy-free gate — the malus itself compounds through the Str/Agi/Tou inheritance with no math, gen 4 copying gen 3's already-dinged values); muse gen ≥ 2 → the new statue loses 1d3 from ONE random stat of Str/Agi/Tou (random-single shape proposed + go-ahead'd at the sitting; 1d3 size + floor-at-1 clamp = CLAUDE dials, flag if the decay rate feels off), `[SHAPE]` "copy decay" log line, player-only message "Some of the likeness is lost in the copying." (DRAFT, rides the release text pass). Inert expired statues already fail the muse picker — only living statues feed the loop, no code needed. `[verify]` next boot: carve creature → statue (gen 1, clean) → copy it (gen 2, clean, stats identical) → copy again (gen 3: one stat visibly down 1-3 on the statue's sheet, decay message in the log, [SHAPE] line names stat + roll); lineage stats never fall below 1. ("stoneshaper snaps to a target creature in sight, but the second part of the cast doesn't snap to walls, requiring manual targeting"). BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (polish sitting; compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified — active next restart).** Recon findings (decomp `IComponent.PickDestinationCell` + `PickTarget.ShowPicker`): the old call started the cursor at the CASTER's cell (Snap only hunts hostiles, useless for walls), and ShowPicker's `UseTarget=true` default silently discards any start coords in favor of the sidebar target — a creature, never a wall — so no PickDestinationCell arguments could fix it. Build: the NPC path's `FindCarvableWallCellFor` scan generalized to `FindCarvableWallCell(origin, radius, RequireVisible)` (identical eligibility walk — solid/no-holo-walltrap-forcefield/no-refusal-tag/audited-props/type-within-level — one predicate now serves both paths); player picker seeds at the nearest player-visible carveable wall to the MUSE (`PLAYER_WALL_SEED_RADIUS = 20`, CLAUDE dial) via direct `PickTarget.ShowPicker` with `UseTarget: false`, caster-cell fallback when no eligible wall is in range (old behavior exactly). `[TF-VERIFY] [SHAPE]` logs the seed cell. `[verify]` next boot: cast Stoneshaper near walls — after picking the muse, the wall cursor starts ON a legal wall near it; cast in open ground — cursor starts on the caster as before; the sidebar-target hijack gone (cursor no longer starts on your combat target).
- **Balance rationale (user, 2026-07-21):** statues are deliberately weaker than Beguiling — temporary by design, copies without inventory; "a survivable buddy who helps with a couple fights." Recorded as the design intent.
- [x] `[design]` ~~**Open tension with the above:** level 10 currently grants a *permanent* statue (until destroyed). Does the "naturally de-animates, inherently temporary" intent override the Lv10 permanence capstone, or is permanence the intended Lv10 exception? User ruling needed.~~ **ANSWERED by the Lv10-permanence ruling two entries below (kept as capstone) — this note predated it.**
- [x] `[design]` **Natural weapons — RULED 2026-07-21: cosmetic middle ground. BUILT** (and repaired live 2026-07-23 — see "Raised in live-fire" below). Statue natural weapons copy the muse's weapon **tile, name, and slot only**; damage/to-hit stay on the mutation's level formula (`GetNaturalDamage`/`GetToHitBonus`). Implement in `ApplyStatueBuffs`: when the replaced part had a DefaultBehavior, copy its Render tile + DisplayName onto the haymaker instance; fall back to gravelly haymaker cosmetics when it had none. (Survey verdict that killed wholesale-keep: disease/poison/tech-organ leaks — see below.)
- [x] `[design]` **Lv10 permanence — RULED 2026-07-21: kept as capstone.** Rewards investment; duration ceasing to matter past 10 is accepted.
- [x] `[bug]` `[design]` **Inert container statue — RULED 2026-07-21, unified spec. BUILT** (all three paths; in-game verification tracked in the checklist at the bottom). One shared helper: *render a creature down into an inert, lootable statue* (fresh non-animated statue object with the creature's render/name/description; all possessions — inventory + equipped — transferred into it; lootable via Inventory + the base game's container part — verify exact part name against e.g. the Chest blueprint at implementation; contingency if containers prove hard: drop a gear pile beside the statue). Serves three paths:
  1. ✔ *Gorgon petrify* (kill path) — **implemented 2026-07-21:** shared helper `_StatueHelpers.cs` (`EntombPossessions`/`ReturnPossessions`, Chest-verified Container+Inventory recipe, all APIs precedent-checked); `Petrify()` entombs before `Die()` and unwinds if the death is prevented.
  2. *Stoneshaper natural expiry (Lv 1–9):* statue no longer reverts to a wall — it freezes into an inert container statue where it stands, lent gear entombed but recoverable. Wall-restore code and `CurWallBp` bookkeeping retire. **Accepted cost: consumed walls never come back.** Fixes the equipped-gear-obliterated bug by design.
  3. *Permanent-statue swap (Lv 10+) — NEW, ruled:* casting with a living permanent statue no longer blocked; the old statue de-animates via the same helper (gear entombed, lootable) and the new one is carved. Normal `BASE_COOLDOWN` applies per cast. Temporary statues (Lv 1–9) remain un-swappable — the lifetime-as-cooldown display already gates recasting naturally.
  4. *Swap confirmation (ruled 2026-07-21):* player-only `Popup.ShowYesNo` before a swap that de-animates a living permanent statue, naming the statue in the prompt (Sunder Mind's self-target confirm is the house precedent). Extra stakes: permanent statues keep their `Leveler` part — they gain levels over time, which a swap destroys unrecoverably; say so in the prompt wording if it fits.

## Geomancy

*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md: Small fixes / De-Harmony investigation)*

- [ ] `[bug]` **Wall HP no longer visible when throwing a rock — USER FLAG 2026-08-12 (their words: "at some point, the harmony patch we used for geomancy stopped letting us see wall HP when a rock is thrown"). SNIFF-OUT RUN same day (user: "we should sniff out the geomancy wall health thing"; context supplied: ava's patch existed because "the 'structural scanning' part had to attach to a player to work" — confirmed, see below). STATIC ANALYSIS EXONERATES EVERY LAYER; DIAGNOSTIC PROBE BUILT + DEPLOYED (`tfgeoscan`, compiles green, hash-verified); root cause NOT yet pinned.** The flag-time theory (readout died with the deleted throw patch) is DEAD — git shows the throw patch was already native by the revival baseline, and the SCANNING patch (`Scanning.HasScanningFor(GameObject, GameObject)` postfix, force-true for objects carrying `Cleo_TerraFirma_PartGeomancyScan`) is STILL IN THE TREE, byte-identical since the ava-era 1.1 backup. Everything checks out statically: patch target signature alive in the current decomp · the HP readout (`XRL.Rules.Strings.WoundLevel/WoundLevelN` — the Look/Sidebar/tooltip surface) calls EXACTLY the patched overload · walls carry no `NoDamageReadout` (WorldTerrain only) · Gemstone merge + `IsGemstone` tag intact, base `Gemstone` root alive · engine auto-applies mod Harmony patches (`ModInfo.ApplyHarmonyPatches`, failures = MODERROR) and the log shows none. Ava's design constraint confirmed from decomp: scanning is player-side int properties (`StructureScannerEquipped` etc.), and the new `GetScanTypeEvent` only picks scan TYPE — there is STILL no native object-side grant, so the patch remains the only per-object shape. **Prime suspect, untestable statically: JIT INLINING** — `HasScanningFor(who, obj)` is a one-line delegator (now routed through `GetScanTypeEvent.GetFor`), prime inlining bait; an inlined call site never sees a Harmony detour, and an engine/Unity update flipping that decision produces exactly "stopped at some point" with zero mod changes. Also noted in passing: `doScan` is GEMSTONE-gated in BOTH eras (plain rocks never scanned — if the remembered behavior was on plain rocks, the memory is of gemstone throws). **`tfgeoscan` (run by hand, not tfbatch): attaches the scan part to the nearest wall (Duration 50 for leisurely eyeballing) and asks the question three ways — compiled call / REFLECTION call (never inlined; answers "did the patch apply at all") / `Strings.WoundLevel` output (the actual display string). Matrix in the wish doc: reflect FALSE = patch never applied · reflect TRUE + bare WoundLevel = INLINING CONFIRMED · reflect TRUE + numeric WoundLevel = engine chain fine, bug is upstream (part not attaching on live gemstone throws — next probe is a real throw).** Fix shapes on deck once the probe names the channel (both CLAUDE, unruled): (a) re-aim the postfix at `Strings.WoundLevelN`/`WoundLevel` themselves (big methods, won't inline; still Harmony); (b) NATIVE de-Harmony shape — while any DoScan reveal is live, Geomancy grants the caster the engine's own `StructureScannerEquipped` (+Bio for creatures) property and revokes at expiry; kills a Harmony patch per the standing direction, cost = scan readouts apply to ALL visible walls/creatures during the window, not just the revealed radius (scope-widening = design call, user's). ~~`[verify]` next boot: `tfgeoscan` beside a wall, read the verdict line + look at the wall.~~ **PROBE RAN 2026-08-12 (user, same session): compiled=True, reflected=True, WoundLevel NUMERIC on a Shale wall, and the user eyeballed EXACT HP in the look UI — ENGINE CHAIN FINE. Inlining hypothesis DEAD; patch applies and is consulted; display renders. The bug is cornered in the ATTACH PATH on real throws: relay not firing vs doScan rolling false vs the wall-attach loop missing.** **LIVE THROWS SAME SESSION (watcher-confirmed): EB sculpted stones land with `scan=False` — BY DESIGN (the `doScan` gemstone gate is verbatim-identical in the ava-era 1.1 backup; plain-rock throws have NEVER attached the scan part in any era) — and `Rough Agate` lands with `scan=True`, relay firing on every throw. VERDICT: NOT A BUG — a DESIGN GAP.** The user's play loop (EB stones at walls) never had a scan in it; the remembered rock-throw wall HP was either gemstone throws or a pre-gate engine behavior (old assembly unavailable, immaterial — fix is the same). Remaining eyeball: user confirms exact HP visible on a wall inside the agate's reveal radius (log half green; tfgeoscan already proved the display chain, so this is expected-yes). **RULING OWED (user's), options on record: (1) keep as-is — gemstones are the scanner, matches shipped text, zero cost · (2) CLAUDE LEAN — rocks scan WALLS only (structural readout on any stone throw; creatures stay gemstone-only; preserves the gemstone premium, restores the readout to the actual play loop; small `OnStoneThrown` change splitting doScan walls-vs-creatures at the attach loop; text consequence: description's gemstone line needs a user rewrite) · (3) all stones scan everything — flattens the gemstone niche, not advocated.**
- [x] `[polish]` ~~Restyle `GetDescription`/`GetLevelText` to base-game voice during the audit pass.~~ **STALE — closed 2026-08-12:** superseded by the 2026-08-06 voice audit (Geomancy GREEN/signed off, Carnivorous pattern) and the status board's text-pass ✔.

## Calcify

*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md: Small fixes)*

### Design calls
- [x] `[design]` **Whorling stones — RULED 2026-07-21: CUT entirely.** Deflection is now exclusively Earthen Barrage's identity (Gravel Whorl); Calcify is purely the wall mutation. Implemented: `Effect_WhorlingStones.cs` deleted; Calcify's `AssocEffect` field, `EndTurnEvent` handler, deflect helper/const, and level-text lines removed. Bonus cleanup: Calcify's vestigial unused `IModEventHandler<AfterSuccessfulThrowEvent>` interface + using removed (would have broken the de-Harmony batch).
- [ ] `[design]` **Balance target — RULED: par with Force Wall, a bit stronger for the investment.** Force Wall ground truth (decompiled): mental, 3 MP, 9 forcefields fixed at all levels, duration 14+2×Lv (34 at 10), cooldown 100, owner can shoot missiles OUT through it. Calcify: physical, 2 MP, 3+Lv/2 walls (3→8), **permanent**, 500×ceil(Lv/2) HP, cooldown 90, opaque (blocks own missiles too), adjacent-start placement only, Stoneshaper can carve its walls. Assessment: Calcify is count-weaker early, decisively stronger late (permanence + HP scale). **RULED + implemented: `BASE_WALLS_CREATED` 3 → 4** (4 at Lv1, exactly 9 at Lv10 — matching Force Wall's count at full investment, in permanent stone). Otherwise numbers stand.
- [x] `[bug]` **MOOTED by the 2026-08-06 true-copy rebalance (next entry): walls now keep native AV/DV and the level text claims neither.** ~~Calcify level text vs wall reality~~ (user flag 2026-07-24, verify session: "calcify description is inaccurate - spawned walls have -10 DV. 0 AV as expected, though this makes them substantially weaker than base walls (10 AV). not sure if i want to flag this as a bug or intended").** Recon split: **AV 0 is INTENDED and stated** (level text: "Created walls have 0 AV and 0 DV"; the design trades armor for the scaled HP pool, 500×⌈Lv/2⌉ — weaker than base walls on purpose; the HP is the defense). **The DV half is a real mismatch:** the text promises 0 DV but created walls keep the base wall blueprint's universal −10 DV (nothing overrides it). Fix either way at the batch: (a) override DV→0 at creation to honor the promise, or (b) correct the text to −10 (every wall in Qud is −10; "0 DV" was always an odd promise for a wall). Claude lean: (b), text follows reality.
- [ ] `[design]` **Fugue-clone Calcify walls outlive the clone (user live-fire flag 2026-07-24, verify session 2: "temporal fugue calcify walls remain after the fugue clone dissipates") — RECON DONE, BUILD SHAPE READY, awaiting ruling.** As-is, a temporary clone leaves PERMANENT walls (free wall economy from a duration ability; also fires via the blurring rock's fugue clone). Decomp-verified build shape: fugue clones carry the `FugueCopy` string property (`TemporalFugue.CreateFugueCopyOf`, line 299); Calcify's wall placement checks `ParentObject.HasStringProperty("FugueCopy")` and, when true, attaches the engine's own `ExistenceSupport` part (`SupportedBy` = the casting clone, `ValidateEveryTurn` = true) to each wall — walls dissipate the moment their maker does, normal casts untouched. Options if ruled differently: Temporary-with-duration instead (walls linger a fixed time after the clone), or keep persistence as-is (Qud-weird freebie). Claude lean: ExistenceSupport — "walls as temporal as their maker." **RULED the lean 2026-08-05 (user, beta-schedule review: "sounds fine") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (compiles clean, hash-verified — active next restart):** `PlaceWall` in Mutation_Calcify.cs checks the caster for the engine's `FugueCopy` string property and attaches `ExistenceSupport` (SupportedBy = the clone, ValidateEveryTurn) to each wall it raises — clone dissipates, walls follow; normal casts untouched; the blurring rock's fugue clone rides the same property for free. CALC cast log gains "fugue-tethered" when it fires. `[verify]` Friday boot: fugue-clone a Calcify caster, have the clone wall off, watch the walls vanish when the clone expires; a normal cast's walls persist as ever.
- [x] `[design]` **NPC-caster gap — RULED batch-now + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23** (user un-parked it: "let's knock calcify off the list"). Two halves: (1) AI advertisement on `AIGetDefensiveAbilityListEvent` + `AIGetRetreatAbilityListEvent` ONLY — deliberately never offensive, because calcified stone is opaque both ways (Force Wall advertises offensively only because its owner can shoot out through its own fields); threat = the event's target, else Force Wall's nearest-visible-hostile retreat scan, passed through `E.Add` so the command carries it. (2) NPC cell-picking `PickNpcWallLine`: perpendicular line across the approach direction, anchored at the caster's adjacent cell (honoring the walls-start-adjacent rule), extended along both orthogonals to the wall count; occupied cells skipped by the placer — partial lines accepted like partial player casts. `[verify]` wish Calcify onto a creature and watch it wall off its attacker when pressed (the appendix "NPC Fissure/Gorgon casters" item gains a calcifier sibling).
- [ ] `[design]` **True-copy walls — RULED 2026-08-06 (beta rebalance pass, item 1; user: "calcify should fully copy the wall it's adjacent to if possible, including description and stats. it should no longer gain HP scaling per level") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** Walls are now full copies of their source blueprint: native HP, AV, DV, and description all stand; level buys count only (4 → 9). Supersedes: the 500×⌈Lv/2⌉ HP formula (`GetWallHp` retired), the 2026-07-25 0-AV/-10-DV ratification (walls keep real armor now), and the HP clause of the par-with-Force-Wall ruling above (count parity at Lv10 stands; the defense dial moved from level to geology — sandstone 150 HP/AV 8, shale 200/10, limestone 1000/15, slate 3000/25, granite 26000/40). `Cleo_TerraFirma_CalcifyWallPart` reduced to a marker (the "calcified" name prefix + the fugue-tether anchor); its shift fields survive for pre-rebalance saves. Fallback blueprint (no natural stone anywhere) rides its Sandstone inherit, 150 HP/AV 8, and carries the old bespoke description in XML now — CLAUDE DIAL, flag if the floor feels wrong. New strings DRAFT for signoff: level-text line "Walls are true copies of the local stone" and ability-card sentence "Creates N walls, true copies of the local stone." `[verify]` cast beside shale and beside limestone: look shows the source stone's description + real stats under the "calcified" prefix; fallback zone shows gray stone at 150 HP.
- [ ] `[design]` **Every-level lattice + scaling cooldown — RULED 2026-08-06 (user flag: "I need to make sure every level up increases something on the mutations, including on calcify"; on the cooldown proposal: "we can try out cool down") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** True-copy left Calcify's odd levels dead (walls tick on evens only; the retired HP formula had covered odds), and the audit confirmed it was the ONLY leveled mutation with dead levels (Gorgon/EB/Stoneshaper/Fissure all move something every level). Fix: cooldown now `max(100 − 10×⌈Lv/2⌉, 50)` — 90 at Lv1-2 (Lv1 unchanged), then 80/70/60/50 stepping on each odd level; evens keep buying the wall. Base precedent for declining cooldowns, decomp-verified: Burgeoning 115−10L, Teleport Other 115−10L, Belcher 210−10L, Sticky Tongue 22−L, Burrowing 15−L. The old "permanent product ⇒ flat cooldown" line was a CLAUDE recommendation (redoubt-numbers-draft) premised on level buying wall HP — premise dissolved with true-copy, provenance checked before superseding. Level text cooldown line now rules-wrapped (it scales). Framed as TRY-OUT — feel-session revisit expected. **Companion ruling, general (user): "1 cost and defect mutations don't level" — Geomancy and Lithovore exempt from the every-level rule by design.** `[verify]` level a calcifier 1→10: ability card cooldown reads 90/90/80/80/70/70/60/60/50/50 while wall count reads 4/5/5/6/6/7/7/8/8/9.

### Stonefrog — NEW unique creature (user concept 2026-07-22; recon DONE, numbers drafted, SETTLE NEXT SESSION — nothing built)
**Concept (user):** not a statue of an existing creature — an original stonefrog. Frog's anatomy, looks like a rock, jumps to attack enemies. **Ruled so far:** role = **BOTH** fall-table ally (DropRock brain-branch, joins ape/statuette) AND wild cave hazard (dynamic encounter tables); **tongue = fully OUT** (ceiling-tongue outside engine without abstraction — user's own call; Sticky Tongue mutation + tongue lash exist but are "Very invasive" for a monster like this — user hesitant, skipped).
**Recon (decompiled/grepped, all verified):** `Frog` anatomy exists (Bodies.xml:349); `BaseFrog` (Str 12/Agi 16/Tou 12, DarkVision, ColdResistance −25, Frogs faction, full frog VO) with family anchors — ice frog L12/HP30/AV3 (+1d10 bite), svardym (jump+charge kit), Frog Golem (constructed-frog precedent). **Jump-attacks are native AI:** `Acrobatics_Jump` skill answers AIGetMovement/Offensive/RetreatAbilityList — two XML lines buy leap-at/leap-in/leap-away; svardym adds `Tactics_Charge` for the pounce. **Disguise depths:** (a) flavor-only render, XML; (b) **stone camouflage — Claude recommends**: `FoliageCamouflage`/`ICamouflage` pattern re-keyed to the mod's `IsAnyStone` tag → +DV while among rocks (serves BOTH roles: ally hides in your fissure fallout, wild one lurks in cave rubble), C#-small (one effect + trigger); (c) true ambush mimic (`Hidden`/`HiddenRender`, renders as an inert rock until it strikes) — most work, serves only the wild role, needs deeper recon. **DISGUISE DEPTH UNRULED.**
**Numbers draft (ALL Claude, unratified):** band 2 weight 5 (statuette's neighborhood); L8 / HP 25 / AV 5 (frog ~3 + stone-body +2); stone rules applied per standing ruling (AcidResistance −50, NoBleed); **cold weakness removed** (BaseFrog −25 is flesh-frog; stone doesn't freeze — flag); kit = Acrobatics_Jump + Tactics_Charge; stone bite 1d6 (stone-mimicry rule); camo +2 DV (foliage-camo's own level); **no MentalShield** (living stone animal, not animated statue — beguilable/dominatable, flagged for veto); **corpse = 1 plain fallen rock** (stalagmite Corpse-drop pattern — kill the stonefrog, throw the stonefrog); wild spawn via cave-tier dynamic tables (exact table names to verify at build); VO mix = frog idle/attack + rock hurt/death (croaks like a frog, chips like a stone).
**To settle next session:** disguise depth (menu RESHAPED by the 2026-08-11 mimic recon below — a+/basilisk/Hidden, mix-and-match) · numbers sign-off · MentalShield call · corpse-rock call · cold-weakness call · which dynamic tables + wild rarity.
**Disguise-depth signal (user, 2026-08-11 roster sitting — NOT a ruling):** "still considering rock frog that disguises itself as a stone before breaking camouflage and jumping" — reads as the true ambush mimic. Cost note: Hidden's "needs deeper recon" caveat is now mostly PAID — the sprouting-orb decomp (same sitting, trapdoor-spider recon in the roster section) covered Hidden's discovery mechanics (Difficulty check, found-on-step, walkable cell, navigation weights).
**MIMIC RECON 2026-08-11 (user-directed: "check out lithofexes ... and how they do their disguises"; base-game ground truth, decomp + base XML, all verified):** the base game ships TWO stone-mimic patterns, and the user's remembered "spawns with husks and hides among them" is the **Molting Basilisk** (base Creatures.xml:6216, Bethesda Susa lurker), not the lithofex.
- **Lithofex pattern — the render pun, zero code:** no disguise part exists anywhere (grep-verified: only StoneGaze.cs + a saving-throw label mention it). `RandomStatue.SetCreature` copies the depicted creature's Tile/RenderString onto every statue and paints it gray, so "gray creature tile" MEANS statue on a Qud screen — and the lithofex renders `&y`/`Y`, statue-gray, among the real statues its own StoneGaze kills accrete (victims become `Random Stone Statue` objects seeded with a DeepCopy; 50/50 pray-at/desecrate Shrine — the garden lures you close). Glance lies, examine is honest ("lithofex" on look).
- **Molting Basilisk pattern — full identity theft + self-seeded decoys (`MoltingBasilisk.cs`):** (1) on first EnteredCell during zone build it scatters **7–11 `Molting Basilisk Husk` items** at random empty cells — solid, occluding, RenderLayer 6, same tile/colors as the live creature; (2) `SyncState()` keyed on Target==null flips its ENTIRE presentation — DisplayName becomes "molting basilisk husk", Description becomes the husk item's text verbatim, `HideCon=1` suppresses the con readout (the one UI tell of a live creature) — so it lies at glance AND examine until it acquires a target, then everything snaps back (state flip, not a spawn); (3) brain = `Hostile=false Wanders=false Hibernating=true` kill radius 1/1 — motionless sleeper, adjacency killer; (4) bite = 3d3 vibro `StoneOnHit` (the quartz-fake petrifies you into its own aesthetic). Resync hooks: AfterObjectCreated / BeginTakeAction-until-created / AICombatStart.
- **Reshaped depth menu for the frog (CLAUDE framing, options not rivals — mix-and-match):** **(a+)** rigorous palette costume — old depth (a) done exactly: frog wears a real mod rock's tile + colors, ambush-at-a-glance for zero C# (the lithofex lesson: the palette IS the mechanic) · **(basilisk)** SyncState-style identity theft — name/desc/HideCon flip until a target exists; ~40 lines of part code, base-game-proven; frog's structural gift over the basilisk: its decoy is a GENERIC ROCK and the mod already carpets floors with those (fissure fallout, spent EB ammo, the ruled corpse-rock — the player seeds the ambush themselves); optional basilisk-style zone-build rock scatter guarantees cover in clean caves · **(Hidden)** the sprouting-orb machinery — object absent until found-on-step; recon paid but returns to being the spider's alone if unused here.
- **STONEFROG PARKED 2026-08-15 (user: "let's not worry about stonefrog yet") — the settle sitting is not in this pass; everything below stays a lean until it happens.** **CLAUDE LEAN, UNRULED (supersedes the earlier "(a+) plus Hidden" lean from the same conversation): (a+) + basilisk flip, no Hidden.** The user's own signal phrase — "disguises itself as a stone before BREAKING camouflage" — is literally the SyncState flip; and a rock you can SEE but can't trust beats an object you can't see (Hidden's whole effect) for this creature's fantasy. Pounce = `Acrobatics_Jump` (native AI leap-at, already in the numbers draft) replacing the basilisk's wait-for-adjacency. Ecology note worth protecting at the ruling: EB players carpet fight scenes with exactly the litter a stonefrog hides in — the mimic depth and the ruled corpse-rock reinforce each other ("dead stonefrogs become the cover for live ones").

**Standing facts that outlive the thread (numbers-sitting REQUIRED READING):**
- **Deft-shadowing:** Deft Throwing's skill-line close-accuracy = 50; engine takes max(item-line, skill-line) → the bracer's +25 (even paired +50) is INVISIBLE to Deft holders. Bracer as drafted = early-game pre-skill item; the final Bonus pick must know this.
- **Cap constraint:** item-line close-accuracy sources SUM; totals past 100 flip the aim-variance multiplier negative. Any future granter (the chalk pouch is first in line) MUST use the cap-aware lend/return idiom — `Part_CobblefurBracer.cs` is the reference.
