## Quarriers in the wild (side-content workstream 3 — ACTIVE 2026-07-22)

**IN SCOPE FOR 1.0 — RULED 2026-07-26 (the release-scope conversation): "unfortunately i'd
like to fit quarriers in the 1.0 release, it's just going to be tricky to implement, i
think."** Quarriers is a release pillar, not a post-1.0 headline; sub-scope within the
workstream (which pieces can trail to 1.1) still open. **Art bar RULED same exchange:
"i'm fine reusing tiles and items for the quarriers. some things will need their own
sprites, but i'm gradually ticking away at those"** — reuse is the 1.0 default, bespoke
sprites land as the user produces them (see design/asset-debt-tracker.md).

**CHALK BORDER SPRITE MADE 2026-08-11 (user: "i also made the sprite for the chalk border, so we could test out quarried stone sites later")** — the block-yard recipe's scaling chalk-line border (`design/recipes.md`, the "chalkline" legend entry; parameterized territory marker for quarried stone sites). **LANDED same day, evening drop: full 8-direction set (`chalklineN/NE/E/SE/S/SW/W/NW.png`) in `TerraFirma/Textures/`** — wire-up = blueprint Render + the yard recipe at the painter port (directional tiles mean the border can corner properly, one blueprint per facing or a facing-aware Render pick at build).

**Painter scope RULED 2026-07-26 (same conversation): "ideally we could have the canyon
painter fully realized in 1.0 with additional items and scenes for flavor added in later
on."** The painter SYSTEM ships complete in 1.0 — carve + drape, camps, lumps, canyon
wiring, the recipe primitive working end-to-end; the CONTENT LIBRARY (scene recipes,
dressing items, tableaus) is open-ended by design and grows post-release.

**1.0-core / trail docket (drafted at the same conversation — core list is CLAUDE
PROPOSAL endorsed in direction, two scope calls still OPEN):**
- **CORE 1.0:** packs in canyons with working mutation AI (the four-handler sitting) ·
  camps via the painter, fully realized · 3x3 lumps · faction entry + neutral reception +
  water ritual on the overseer · ~~provisioner trade~~ **vine-merchant trade in camps
  (2026-08-11 relocation — packs no longer trade)**.
- **TRAIL (post-1.0 flavor, per the painter ruling):** cairn/musing tableaus · geode
  lottery · dromad ride-alongs · additional scene recipes and dressing items.
- **Both scope calls RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (same conversation):** **Mole-beast — IN 1.0,
  blocked on the user's creature concept, not on scope:** "the mole beast's design
  *settles* the weirdness question right. i have to come up with a creature for it and i
  just haven't yet. it's annoying but it'll come with time." No depth fork exists —
  whatever creature the user invents IS the design; nothing to build until it lands.
  **Stilt merchant — ~~IN 1.0~~ BENCHED 2026-08-11 (vine sitting, user: "the stilt
  merchant can be benched till a later date?" — supersedes this IN-1.0 ruling):** the
  original shape was "easy inclusion i think - it's just provisioner with a tent
  attached" (reuse the provisioner blueprint + a StiltTents table entry) — that shape
  dies with the provisioner's trade role moving to the vine merchant (see Creature
  roster). Reopen later; a Claude float (potted transplanted cutting) is on file there.
- **Writing sittings sheet (user OK'd 2026-07-26): `design/writing-sittings-1.0.md`** —
  every user-owed text debt under the sign-off policy, consolidated for burn-down.

Two committed concepts (user, ruled): **(1)** quarrier packs in the canyons carrying 3x3 lumps of rock they've carved free from the walls; **(2)** a Stilt bazaar merchant selling non-expiring fissure stones as a random tent spawn — cheap, one-use utility grenades to stock up on.

**Lore direction (user, ruled):** the stonecarvers came from outside Qud (why they're a mod, not basegame). No established holds — at most a small temporary camp they stop at on pilgrimages through Qud, drawn by its rich canyons and deep caves full of varied stone. They bring their culture, their mutations (the mod's seven), and a new lens on the game. Consequence: no procedural-village surface; everything is zone-build-time content that reaches existing saves in unvisited zones.

### Rulings
- [x] `[design]` **NPCs use their mutations — RULED 2026-07-22.** Any mutation a stonecarver type carries, its AI must actually cast — "gotta be able to fight back" against a mutant who opens fire on them. Scope consequence: whichever mutations land on the roster commit their AI work (Fissure: done; EB: advertisement handler + NPC toggle handling; Stoneshaper: NPC branch for the player-only cell-picking path + wall-and-muse targeting, the most complex). These handlers are shared infrastructure — the parked "wild creatures with innate mutations" design-budget item inherits them for free.
- [x] `[design]` **Reception — RULED 2026-07-22: neutral at baseline, trending positive.** At the start you're just another wandering adventurer — they don't start combat; at minimum they can trade with you. Implies a proper faction entry (neutral standing needs a home faction; killing quarriers for their lumps costs rep — the temptation loop is intended). **AMENDED 2026-08-11 (vine sitting): the trade half relocates — wandering packs are neutral-NO-trade; trade lives in camps on the vine merchant (see Creature roster). Neutrality, faction entry, and the temptation loop all stand unchanged.**
- [x] `[design]` **Water ritual — RULED 2026-07-22: yes.** Engine hook verified: `GivesRep` part on the legendary blueprint (Snapjaw Hero 0 precedent) puts the faction on the reputation screen, makes kills cost rep, and makes the legendary water-ritual-capable.
- [x] `[design]` **Pack structure — RULED 2026-07-22: legendary-pack shape.** Several distinct stonecarver types divvying up the work (variety is the point), one legendary leading them — exactly the base game's wild-legendary idiom. Verified: `SnapjawParty1` = pickeach roles at varied chances + `Snapjaw Hero 0` at Chance 3; a `-with-Warlord` guaranteed-leader variant table exists as precedent; hero blueprint = base-role inherit + hero `builder` (name/stat boosts) + `GivesRep` + `Role=Hero` tag.
- [x] `[design]` **Legendary naming — RULED 2026-08-14 (naming session, user: "i feel like this works and we can bank it"): bespoke `Naming.xml` style, NOT the default.** Do-nothing fallback verified = `Qudish` style (scope `Genotype="Mutated Human"` Priority 50) — legendaries would read as *locals*, against the across-the-Moghra'yi identity. Shape ruled: **etruscan-chopped personal name + english material-craft byname** (source box: mysteriousetruscans.com/names.html, user-supplied; etruscan = isolate language known from tomb inscriptions — the resonance is the point). Engine ground truth (decomp `NameStyle.cs:221`, `Grammar.cs:114`, 2026-08-14): **the generator has NO phonotactic filter** — sole guard is an 8-entry slur blocklist with a 1000-reroll loop (fails loud: name becomes `NameGenBadWordsFail{n}`); all base-game quality control is bank curation, so scrubbed banks are the defense, by construction.
  **Personal banks (ACCEPTED as-drafted 2026-08-14; junction architecture: C-final prefixes + VCV infixes ×0–1 + C-initial postfixes = every seam C/V, pileups impossible):**
  - prefixes ×12: `ar vel lar than ram sem cul tles pev ras mun spur`
  - infixes ×13: `utha athi eni ena ari ithi aru eli` + **five adds 2026-08-15 (user-blessed after demo): `anthi elthu arna uni avi`** — VCCV pieces restoring the th-cluster growl mid-name where junctions stay safe (elthu = the Velthur treasure rehomed away from its Arthur problem; uni = the attested chief goddess; avi = attested "years," the tomb-inscription word). Repetition dial noted: doubles live in the no-infix space — shipping `InfixAmount="1"` or `"0-2"` is the lever, a length-taste call at build.
  - postfixes ×9: `tha thia na nia ce za til sa cna`
  - Crime sweep: full 108 two-piece enumeration eyeballed clean. Removed in drafting: `ar+thur`=Arthur (thur cut), `vin+ce`=Vince (vin→mun), `se`/`her` (Secure/Here/Hera), `au` (Auth), bare `n` (triple-n). **Flagged, not vetoed, at bless — standing unless user reopens:** Arsa (`ar+sa`, rude-adjacent), Ramza (`ram+za`, FF Tactics), `ithi+thia` seam stutter (rare), overall register softer than early drafts (growly `nth`/`th` postfixes died in the scrub; th lives on in `tha`/`thia`).
  - Feature, not bug: banks can resurrect attested names whole — Lartha, Larce, Rasce, Pevtha, Tlesna (real family name). User-enjoyed.
  **Byname banks (user-curated to 12×12; sized for base-game parity — base hero title/epithet banks run 4–13 values, snapjaw = 7×6):**
  - materials: `stone gravel cobble spall scree flint grit cairn quern karst lode salt`
  - crafts: `smith augur wright knapper reeve ward delver graver scribe reader carter setter`
  - Curation rulings (user, 2026-08-14): **steward→ward** (Woodward/Hayward surname formation; distinguished from the 07-22 warder rejection — embedded byname, not a standalone role label next to base wardens) · **gaffer cut** · **tooth→setter** (bank went all-occupational; the body-epithet register exited the random banks entirely) · **hands reserved out of the random bank** pending the easter-egg call. Register notes on record: augur = the actual etruscan national craft (disciplina etrusca); salt = the Moghra'yi crossing; quern ↔ the mod's stone mill.
  - **TWO-AXIS AMENDMENT — RULED 2026-08-15 (same sitting as name-drives-kit; user direction: "steal the combinative part of goatfolk heroes where the clan has an effect, for our prefixes"). The byname's MATERIAL half now grants a perk (the clan-effect axis) while the CRAFT half grants the kit — one compound word is the whole character sheet.** Builder tools all precedented in `GoatfolkHero1`: `BoostStat` (AV is a stat — rides it fine), resist stats, `Stat.Random(1,2)` rolled amounts (Clovenhorn's horn level), multi-grant, gear populations. Bank sizes amended from the 08-14 12×12:
    - **materials — FINAL 13 (user directive 2026-08-15: parity with the craft bank, wide stat variety; an interim 8-word tightening lived and died the same sitting — synonym-debt dissolved once every word carried a distinct perk):** `stone flint scree cairn grit chalk spall marl cobble salt karst quartz lode`. Permanent cuts: gravel, whet + **quern→grit swap (user-ruled)** — grit's word *is* its perk (grit = english for willpower; the pun teaches the stat); cut vocab lives on in etch-text/description prose. RIP Quernaugur. **ashlar drafted for +DV, user-vetoed ("doesn't slot cleanly") → spall (Claude slate, user-accepted)**.
    - **material→perk table (magnitude discipline: singles ~+2, combos +1/+1 — goatfolk precedent, Stargazer +2 single vs Whitetongue triple +1s):** stone **+1 Str/+1 Tou** (combo; the bruiser pair) · flint +2 Agi · **scree +1–2 AV rolled, PURE — +HP rider CUT (user-ruled)** · **cairn +2 Ego (user cut the MoveSpeed draft; cairns are memory, ego is the water-ritual stat)** · grit +2 Will · chalk +2 Int (the writing stone) · **spall +2 DV** — scree/spall = the two defenses split by how rock fails: scree soaks the hit, spall sheds it · marl +HP (the fertile stone) · cobble +MoveSpeed (the paved-road word — MS landed where it always belonged) · **salt heat 50 (user-ruled)** · **karst cold 50 (user-ruled; Night Vision rider CUT by user)** · quartz elec 50 (piezoelectric — the resist trio = the faction's weather report: sun above, chill below, storm) · lode wealth (richer gear populations — feeds the temptation loop). Non-user-numbered rows are Claude drafts standing unvetoed; numbers finalize at builder implementation.
    - **crafts 12→13:** EB column evened to 3 (user direction: even out, geomancy+office stay low) — **+slinger (Claude word-draft, unvetoed** — the honest ancient stone-missile trade; Gritslinger/Flintslinger). Columns: Fissure 3 (smith/knapper/delver) : Stoneshaper 3 (wright/setter/graver) : EB 3 (ward/carter/slinger) : Geomancy 2 (augur/reader) : office 2 — **split (Claude draft, unvetoed): reeve +Ego/+Will (the commander), scribe +Int/+Will (the lettered one)**. **Word count per column = spawn weight** — the bank is the loot table; weight edits are vocabulary edits.
  - [x] `[design]` **Byname delivery — RULED 2026-08-15: hero epithet, via the goatfolk-builder structure below** (the epithet-vs-`TwoNameChance` fork closed by construction — `GoatfolkHero1` names via `MakeEpithet`).
  - [x] `[design]` **NAME-DRIVES-KIT STRUCTURE — RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "sounds fine to bank"; supersedes the 07-22 overseer mutation-pool ruling's pool boundary).** Ground truth first (decomp `GoatfolkHero1.cs:28-92`): the base game's goatfolk builder rolls the EPITHET FIRST, then a switch on the name text GRANTS what it promises (Stargazer→LightManipulation+INT, Twicetalker→TwoHeaded+horns item, Souldrinker→LifeDrain, Clovenhorn→Horns+Charge skill; clan titles grant too — Clan Sol→PhotosyntheticSkin, Clan Whitetongue→mental stats). Mamon Souldrinker drains life BECAUSE the generator named him Souldrinker. Ours mirrors it: bespoke overseer builder (absorbs the ruled ~20-line picker) rolls the material×craft epithet, then `Contains` on the craft half grants the kit. **The epithet bank IS the pool manifest** — a mutation is rollable iff a byname word vouches for it; user's "every pack mutation + a few base-game guests possible" is delivered by coining words, and the NPCs-use-their-mutations bar becomes the sole gate (a word may not enter the bank until its mutation's NPC AI is committed). Ordering race: none — naming precedes grants inside the builder. Gorgon landing strip: now routes through the bank (give the gaze a word → the two stranded 07-24 verifies get their NPC).
    - **Word↔grant mapping — RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "the word mapping is fine"): the drafted mapping IS the bank; wording stays the user's to polish at the text pass. ONE SLOT DEFERRED by the same ruling — Gorgon's Gaze has no word yet (user: "we can decide a word for gorgon's gaze at some point"), and until it gets one the two stranded 07-24 gorgon verifies still have no NPC to land on.** Mapping as drafted: knapper/delver/smith → Fissure · wright/setter/graver → Stoneshaper · ward/carter/slinger → EB · augur/reader → Geomancy (user's float 2026-08-15 — geomancy = literal earth-divination, `EarthyVibrations` scan) · reeve/scribe → no mutation, office stat-boosts instead (+Ego/+Will class, Clan Whitetongue precedent; user queried reeve→geomancy, Claude counter: office words stay unscoped — kit words vs office words is the grammar, user did not push back). New coinages owed if their mutations join: Calcify (*limewright* floated — lime-burning = the real calcination trade), Gorgon's Gaze (word open), base-game guests (user picks; Two-Headed→Twicetalker is direct precedent).
    - [ ] `[design]` **Geomancy NPC AI cost check** (what does an AI casting a scan even do — possibly near-free, possibly flavorful fight-opener: overseer pings the player through stone). Gate for augur/reader granting.
    - [ ] `[design]` **Calcify NPC AI cost check** — same gate, if it gets a word at all (user's call).
    - [ ] `[design]` **Base-game guest list** (user's picks; zero AI cost — native handlers).
    - [ ] `[design]` **Baseline kit under the grant OPEN:** what the overseer carries BEFORE the epithet adds one (the 07-22 "2-with-chance-for-3" shape survives as base-kit-plus-grant if wanted; goatfolk precedent = hero inherits the shaman's full kit and the epithet tops it up). Sizes the fight; user's.
  - [ ] `[design]` **Stonehands easter egg OPEN** (Clan Sotur descends from "Sotur Stonehands," *Across Moghra'yi* — the only named person from beyond the desert). With the body register gone from random banks, a kept Stonehands would be the faction's ONLY body-byname — the wink got louder. Keep-vs-avoid is a lore call, user's.
  - [ ] `[design]` **Hero TITLE layer undesigned** ("overseer of the ___", snapjaw `the *Adjective* Snapjaw *Occupation*` template idiom; clan-name slot floated — rhymes with Sunderlies cave-clans). Separate from the byname; user's words when it opens.
  - [ ] `[verify at build]` Modded `Naming.xml` merges + our faction-scoped style wins the priority contest for the hero (no reason it wouldn't — smoke test anyway). Mechanics on record: faction-scoped style covers both species variants (one culture, two bodies); `HyphenationChance`/`TwoNameChance` dials; epithet/honorific plumbing exists in `NameStyle.Generate` (`HasEpithet`).

### Open decisions (one at a time)
- [ ] `[design]` **ON THE TABLE (user's question): how do they get the quarried stone *out* of Qud?** Canon recon 2026-07-22 (Books.xml, *Across Moghra'yi* Vols. I–III): the only established overland route in/out of Qud is crossing the Moghra'yi, the Great Salt Desert; the canonical crossing traffic is **dromad merchant caravans** ("accounts given by fungi who crossed Moghra'yi on the backs of dromad merchants"; "O, how the dromads know!"). Named far lands beyond the desert: **the Sunderlies** (Vol. I — "towering sundered bluffs… canyon walls dotted with innumerable cave-mouths… colossal, earthly honeycombs," cave-clan freeholds, Clan Sotur descends from "Sotur Stonehands," sigil = a sundered mountain), **Athenreach** (Vol. II), **Oth, the Free City** (Vol. III). Mechanically nothing ever leaves the map — the answer only needs to manifest in what the player sees (direction of travel, pack-animal entries in the party table, camp contents, conversation text).
  - **User's current thinking (2026-07-22, slept on it, NOT yet ruled):** (1) stonecutters travel with dromad merchants sometimes; (2) they have their own beast of burden for transporting stone — maybe a vehicle with an interior; (3) maybe the beast is a huge mole-like thing that uses its claws to help dig out quarriable stone when it's not carrying it.
  - [x] `[design]` **Mole-beast RULED 2026-07-22: committed.** The stonecarvers' beast of burden is the huge mole-like digger. **No interior** (cut — cargo, not walk-in). **Burrowing via BurrowingClaws: in, but provisional** — may be cut later if it misbehaves (PathAsBurrower is the dial). OPEN: what makes the mole *Qud* — user wants it weirder than a merely-big animal; weirdness axis unruled.
  - **Mole-beast feasibility (2026-07-22, XML ground truth):** no mole-anything in basegame. The only civilized burrowers are the four mopango blueprints (a friendly *pilgrim* people — canon resonance noted) — all carry `BurrowingClaws`, and the AI dial is on the mutation tag itself: `PathAsBurrower="true"` = genuinely tunnels (pathfinder treats walls as diggable), `"false"` = claws without map-eating pathing (mopango use both deliberately). "Huge" = tile art + description + stats; all creatures are one cell, no multi-tile tech. "Digs out quarriable stone" has two altitudes: flavor-only (pure XML) vs mechanically real (small C# hooking its wall destruction to yield rock items — mod already owns wall↔rock machinery). `Interior` composes orthogonally with claws/brain.
  - **Feasibility recon (2026-07-22, XML ground truth):** *Dromad ride-alongs* — wilderness tables seed `DromadTrader` groups at Chance 2; trader blueprint carries a `DromadCaravan` part that conjures the entourage. Shape (a): dromad-trader entry in our own pack table — pure XML, nothing to verify. Shape (b): merge stonecarvers into the caravan entourage — depends on whether `DromadCaravan` is table-driven or hardcoded (one-function decompile check at implementation). *Interior beast* — `Interior` part is XML-attachable to any creature/object (`Cell` → merged `<cell>` in Worlds.xml `Interior` world → `.rpm` map, same tech as camp chunks; mecha cockpit 5x3, Mover atrium 16x14). `Vehicle` part (pilots, power, EMP) is separate and optional — a living beast needs only `Interior`. Caveats: no basegame precedent for an *autonomous wandering* creature with an interior (mechas move piloted, other interiors are stationary) — decompile + smoke-test "interior follows wanderer" and "beast dies: what happens to interior contents" before spec. Design note: stone stacked inside the beast = the temptation loop, one layer deeper.
  - **Beast REVISED 2026-08-07 (carve-jam session, user direction): the mole becomes a giant STONE-EATING CENTIPEDE.** Supersedes the mole body plan in the 2026-07-22 ruling (the beast-of-burden commitment itself stands; BurrowingClaws-for-pathing dial carries over). User's fiction (user owns concept): vast — "potentially spanning a mile" — a walking wagon for the caravan, outfitted with months-if-not-years of supplies from home; quarried stone spread evenly along its back so as not to crush it, doubling as protective-but-breakable armor; mouth = darner dragonfly nymph labium × excavator; legs instead of hands. Stone-eating ties it to the Lithovore biology — dig-is-eat, one organ, one gesture (Claude framing, user-aligned). Carve-signature dials (DRAFT, Claude numbers-territory): wide low bore, sinuous low-frequency weave, walls smoothed with fine parallel leg-striations, ZERO spoil + polished-pebble gastrolith dung as trail (the dung rhymes with the feeding-sty forage note in the room jam).
    - [x] `[design]` **Single tile RULED 2026-08-07 (user: "that's what qud would do")** — star-kraken idiom: vast in fiction, one tile in play, the mile lives in prose. **Claude's head-tile + scenery-segment-chain proposal CUT by this ruling** — no multi-object body, ever. Consequences: cargo-armor = breakable equipped armor and/or inventory death-drops on the one creature (no statue-spill machinery needed); the caravan-robbing temptation stays on pack lumps + the beast's own inventory/armor.
    - [ ] `[design]` **Mouth/weapon OPEN, two user candidates:** (a) face-slot labium-excavator natural weapon (Horns-pattern head-mounted natural + BurrowingClaws dig tag; optional Sticky-Tongue-style pull for the labium shot) — **Claude lean: the dig=eat unification argument**; (b) frontal claw naturals. User's words: "could also see the case for frontal claw natural weapons."
    - [ ] `[design]` **Name OPEN** (user: "not really?") — candidate-slate session on request, naming-session idiom. Direction space floated (Claude): drover's working word / geological word / untranslated home-word (composes with the homeland-mystery dial).
  - **Mole-FOLK species axis — RATIFIED BY BUILDING 2026-08-15 (user chose both bodies at the roster build).** Each role now has a thin mole child: `stonerat.png` + Burrowing Claws, profession unchanged. `PathAsBurrower` is OFF (CLAUDE PICK, flagged): with it on they path THROUGH walls and would tunnel out of a hand-painted warren; the claws still dig and still hit. **Species TAG deliberately left inherited** — building the body does not claim the species string, and what these people ARE stays the user's lore call. Display names "mole hewer / mole mason / mole outrider" are CLAUDE DRAFT, existing only so the two bodies are tellable apart in a log; collapse them to the bare profession on a word. *(Original float, preserved:)*  the stonecarvers themselves partly mole *people* — Barathrumite-idiom mixed faction, "though not uniformly." Mole variants carry BurrowingClaws naturals; human variants keep pickaxes (camps still littered with them). Professions remain the face — species is a second axis under the ruled six-role roster (snapjaw variant-blueprint idiom, cheap). **RESHAPED same session (user: "I think we could live with one sprite for each stonecarver") — species is FIXED PER PROFESSION, not rolled per individual:** each of the six roles commits to one species, one sprite. Consequences: sprite debt stays at six (user's to draw); look-descriptions single-body (text debt unchanged); pack tableau keeps the six-silhouette legibility the profession-first ruling was built for; the mixed faction displays in every pack by construction (a rolled axis could chance into all-human). NEW OPEN (user's, concepts theirs): ~~**which roles are mole-folk**~~ — considerations floated (Claude, unruled): claws pull toward digging trades (hewer; drover has burrower-tends-burrower logic); provisioner-as-human has an outsider-facing-face argument; overseer species = loudest choice (water-ritual face); mason species reads as theology. Claude proposals riding on it, unruled: **hands are cultural, not anatomical** — claw or pick, a mason cuts square (preserves the two-voice carve legibility; the claws' showcase = the soft round spaces: divots, satellites); PathAsBurrower=false on camp residents so claws don't eat the map. **QUESTION COLLAPSED 2026-08-11 (user, sprite sitting): "the 'which job is a mole vs human' collapses with the color distinction — since we have both tiles, we can just do random tile parts on them, and the detail color already communicates the role." Species is ROLLED PER INDIVIDUAL again (un-does the fixed-per-profession reshape now that its six-sprite premise is gone); role stays on DetailColor.** Recon (Claude, same exchange, verified): stock `RandomTile` BUILDER is exactly this — base farmers roll among six tiles (base Creatures.xml:880), so `<builder Name="RandomTile" Tiles="human,mole" />` per role blueprint, zero code. Consequences threaded: the 08-07 "mixed faction displays by construction" guarantee reverts to STATISTICAL (an all-human pack can roll; per-role tile weighting is the dial if it ever grates) · look-descriptions must go BODY-AGNOSTIC (text-pass consequence — role descs can't name the body; the writing-sittings sheet inherits this constraint) · ~~open dial: cosmetic-only vs anatomy-follows-tile~~ **SETTLED 2026-08-11, CONVERGED LEAN (user: "i lean more towards only mole tile blueprints getting burrowing claws personally" + bank OK'd "that sounds fine yeag"; Claude concurring on the merits): ANATOMICAL — mole-tile spawns carry `BurrowingClaws`.** Grounds on record: qud bodies are load-bearing (examine must corroborate the sprite — claw naturals in the weapon list; cosmetic-only would be the mod's one painted-on body) · mopango precedent (base game's only civilized burrowers are anatomically clawed on all four blueprints — species-is-anatomical is how qud writes burrowing peoples). **Implementation correction (Claude, same exchange): `RandomTile` builder can't attach mutations, so the randomness moves to the VARIANT-BLUEPRINT idiom** — each role gets a thin mole child (inherit role base, override tile, add claws, ~6 lines), pack tables roll between variants (snapjaw pattern, zero C#); the builder shape from the collapse note is superseded. Riders unchanged: `PathAsBurrower=false` on camp residents (claws fight as melee naturals — the NPCs-use-their-mutations ruling is satisfied without map-eating) · body-agnostic descs (species still rolls, now at table level). Texture note banked: mole carvers are always-armed (naturals never drop), humans leave pickaxes as loot — the species die differently.
  - **Mopango symmetry (lore note, user insight 2026-08-07 — mopango are PANGOLINS, no mole collision):** mopango = cave-faring pilgrim folk with Light Manipulation who naturally light up wherever they go; the stonecarvers are cave-faring pilgrims whose passage leaves *shape* — carving, sockets, cairns; the beast's trail (bore-mouth + pebble dung) joins the same ladder. Two cultures marking the dark, each in its own medium. The escalation ladder (cairn → musing spot → camp) is the mechanism that delivers this read.

  - [x] `[design]` **KITS BUILT 2026-08-15.** Shape RULED by the user against Claude's fixed-list pitch: *"rather than use player base armor, we could do something like generate tier appropriate armor with a few items biased to roll each time."* That is the base game's own idiom and `HumanoidEquipment` is the model. **The mechanism that makes it work: `{ownertier}`**, a substitution token resolving tier off the OWNING creature, so one table scales itself with no per-tier duplicates. `GetTier()` = explicit `Tier` tag, else `(Level - 1) / 5 + 1`. **Our chassis is Level 10 with no Tier tag, so carvers currently roll TIER 2 gear** — that coupling is now load-bearing, since raising the roster level raises what they wear, and the level is still a Claude placeholder for the numbers sitting. Wired via `<tag Name="InventoryPopulationTable">`, the same tier-child idiom already noted for the snapjaw miner. **Spine (`Cleo_TerraFirma_Quarrier Kit`), certain every time:** blunted chisel + splintered mallet + waterskin + 2-5 torches, then ROLLED tier armor (75%, the `HumanoidEquipment` dynamic/static 40:60 split) and tier shoes (60%). Entries without a `Chance` in a `pickeach` group fire every time, which is the "biased to roll each time" half. **The chisel+mallet pair means the etch gate passes BY CONSTRUCTION**, so the parked idle-etch brain part becomes possible the moment kits exist. **Role tells, one item each** (the mutation is the job, the item is only the tell): **hewer = Vinewood Sap Mask** — chosen over a pickaxe DELIBERATELY, since `DiggingTool` installs Digging and answers `PathAsBurrowerEvent` and an armed hewer would tunnel out of a hand-painted warren; the mask is AV 0, pure characterisation, and says the same thing. **mason = `Cleo_Stonecarver_Maquette`** (RULED by the user, superseding Claude's bubble level: "give the mason a figurine") — the mod's OWN table, the exact one in the player calling kit, so a mason carries what a player stonecarver carries. **outrider = cobblefur bracer**, which drags an ecology in behind it (they hunt cobble baboons for the pelt in the same canyons, and `AddsRep Baboons -100` makes the baboons hate them for it). **Overseer takes the bare spine and nothing bespoke**, per the ruled goatfolk structure: the hero inherits the ordinary kit and the EPITHET tops it up. That is what keeps the byname load-bearing instead of decorative, and it finally gives the `lode` material perk something to bite (richer gear populations, by name alone). Closes the "baseline kit under the grant OPEN" item. **Figurines scoped to the mason only**, not smeared across the pack as a loot floor; a `Figurines {ownertier}` line at low chance is all it would take if that ever changes. **Open, surfaced BY the kit decision:** AV on a Level 10 chassis is soft, and now that gear quality is coupled to level, "soft and numerous vs tougher and fewer" is a real fork rather than a dial — numbers sitting.

  - **Profession-first structure — RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "profession first is fine, it's a qud idiom"; the user reached the idiom argument independently, which was the argument), promoted from Claude lean 2026-07-22 — user had floated provisioner/mason as example roles:** distinguish types by profession noun — the verified basegame idiom for civilized packs (Goatfolk Hornblower/Sower/Yurtwarden/Shaman vs snapjaws' combat nouns; Yurtwarden = identity-is-tending-the-camp precedent). Three layers: profession (face — name/gear/description/camp presence), mutation (engine each profession works with), and `Role` — **NOT an "AI layer": that description was wrong and is corrected here.** **ROLE RECON, decomp ground truth 2026-08-15 (user: "we should investigate creature AI roles more thoroughly? just in case"). Nothing under `XRL.World.Parts` or `XRL.World.AI` reads the tag at all.** It feeds four unrelated systems:
    1. **Difficulty / con rating** (`DifficultyEvaluation.GetRoleLevelOffset`): Minion −5 · Skirmisher −3 · Leader +3 · Summoner +4 · Hero +5 · **everything else 0**. Brute, Artillery, Tank, Controller and Lurker are cosmetic on this path.
    2. **Dynamic-table spawn weight** (`GameObjectFactory.RoleWeightMultipliers`): Common 4.0 · Minion 4.0 · Skirmisher 1.0 · Artillery/Uncommon/Brute/Tank 0.25 · Specialist/Leader/Hero 0.1 · Rare/Epic 0.01. Controller, Lurker, NPC, Breeder and Summoner are absent from the table entirely. **Only bites DYNAMIC tables** — an explicit population entry like our pack table is unaffected.
    3. **XP value** (the `*XP` stat): Minion ×20 · Leader ×100 · Hero ×200 · default ×50, per half level.
    4. **A Minion-only stat penalty**: `Role == "Minion"` decrements `Boost` on ALL SIX attributes. Minion also excludes a blueprint from a `ZoneManager` selection and gets its level QUARTERED in an `IComponent` threat check.
    **Consequence for our roster, caught the same sitting: `mason = Minion` (a Claude draft from that morning) was doing real damage** — statted down across the board, reading five levels weaker than it is, paying 20x XP, and quartered in threat maths, for a profession that is a peer and not a mook. **Changed to `Summoner`, which is mechanically honest AND literally true** (Stoneshaper summons statue companions) and rates +4, so the con warning matches what the fight actually becomes. hewer `Brute` and outrider `Artillery` stand: both are 0-offset flavor, and their 0.25 dynamic weight never applies to explicit tables. Overseer takes `Hero` from the legendary builder (+5, ×200 XP), which is correct. **If real behavioural difference between professions is ever wanted, `Role` is the wrong lever entirely** — that lives in Brain properties and AI goal parts (the `AIThrowAndScoot` the outrider already inherits is the example on file). Why: profession identity holds even where combat AI isn't built yet (mason reads as mason without casting); "provisioner" maps directly onto `GenericInventoryRestocker` (dromad-trader machinery — makes the pack itself the trading stop, cashing in the reception ruling's "at least they can trade"); work-division makes the pack tableau legible at a glance. Roster ruling = name the professions + which mutation each carries.

Recon that framed the call: `interests` is the water-ritual SECRETS trade, and `WillBuy`/`WillSell` are independent, so the buy/sell split is the characterising half (base Farmers' Guild is one buy-only line, `humanoid,settlement`, and it tells you everything about farmers). Tag→prose is a hardcoded switch in `FactionInterest.GenerateDescription` (~70 tags); the grammar is that a creature/faction tag pairs with `lair` or `settlement` to make "the locations of X lairs", which is why those two dominate the base file. A `Description` attribute can override the prose, so invented tags are possible (base does it 3x); `MatchAny` and `Inverse` also exist. **Built: SELL `desertcanyons` + `underground`, BUY `ruins`.** The read: quarriers sell their own domain, the canyons and everything under them, and pay for leads on worked stone they have not reached. **Ruins direction deliberately survives the 2026-08-12 calling change** that swapped ruins lore for canyons — the calling stopped being FROM the ruins; the guild still wants to hear about them. *Unproven caveat kept on record: the tag→prose switch and the buy/sell blurb are decomp-confirmed, but the code that actually OFFERS the secrets is not in the current decomp dump, so "these tags gate which secrets get traded" is the evident reading and not something Claude has watched happen* · feelings ship NEUTRAL to everyone rather than inventing a politics, with only the Girsh/Entropic hostility both base guilds state · **whether a pack should open friendly, wary or hostile to the player is NOT decided** (currently neutral at 0 starting rep).

- **Stonecarvers are MUTANTS, not True Kin — user, firm. NO CALLING WRINKLE (corrected 2026-07-24):** callings ARE the mutant chargen category (`Mutated Human Subtypes="Callings"` / `True Kin Subtypes="Castes"`, Genotypes.xml) — the shipped Stonecarver calling was mutant-side all along. The "True Kin chargen" claim was an error in the old merge note (line ~35, now fixed) that Claude propagated despite a standing memory saying the opposite. Nothing to reconcile; the floated apprentice/rename options are void.
- **Homeland: no hard canon assignment — user ("touchy," not strictly opposed).** Claude pitch, awaiting ruling: the carvers never name their home (diaspora identity — ask a carver where home is and they tap the nearest wall). - **Creed draft (pitched, user "mostly fine"):** stone remembers; carving is waking, not making; Qud's stone is *awake* (the pilgrimage reason); the mutations are the stone answering. Spent-stone rites unify `DullAfter` + the "You return the stone to the earth" reabsorb text as one belief.
- **Geode lottery — user likes (had considered it independently).** Sealed geodes as mystery grab-bags in Stilt-merchant stock; crack → population table roll (mundane rock / brine / gemstone / rare **viewing stone** jackpot).
- **Camp — leaning commit, built manually.** User: base procgen too flimsy to hook; consistent with the ruled "no procedural-village surface." Tech: static chunk skeleton + population tables + placement hints (SixDayTents idiom) — fixed bones, rolled furnishings. Strange-mood tableau (one mason stood "listening" at a raw block, camp working around them) floated as the set-piece.
  - [x] `[design]` **Painter architecture RULED 2026-07-24 (user: "genius — solves a problem I've had since their inception"):** recursive recipe primitive. A recipe = footprint requirement + fixed placements + weighted blanks; a blank resolves to an object, a table roll, or another recipe (nesting dolls). Hard depth cap 3 (camp → room → cluster). Fit rule: try smaller variant, else skip — NO backtracking/solver/adjacency logic ever (that's the flimsy procgen being avoided). Carve-vs-drape is a per-recipe attribute, not two systems. Every recipe logs placed/shrunk/dropped in the TF-VERIFY idiom. Payoff: recipes reuse across contexts (grave = lone corridor cairn AND camp blank — recombination reads as unauthored narrative); strange-mood mason = rare roll in one workshop blank, not a special skeleton. Two independent dials: spawn weight (camp rarity across canyons) vs recipe tables (variety within a camp). Middle ground achieved: authored texture at procedural density — neither one overdesigned static zone nor canyon-spam. Same primitive later serves the Unfinished One, lithovore restaurant, Stilt dressing.

Context: Claude asserted a wrong basic fact (calling=True Kin) the same session it pitched writing an anchor book; the user's standing all-text-is-draft rule exists for exactly this. Implemented as the sprawl table's rare jackpot (the Red Rock Weight-1-shrine idiom): **lone cell** — a single ring unit alone in the wall (carveRanks forced to 1 unit), 8% of sprawl rolls, front-adjacency connects it. Sprawl table rebalanced: nibble 32 / slot 14 / notch 27 / stepped 11 / island 8 / cell 8. Verified 30 zones: 5 lone cells (~1 in 6 zones), 0 sealed. The register spectrum is now complete: sprawl bites (accumulated work) → lone cells (hermit) → comb (organic warren) → ranks (planned settlement) — one vocabulary, four social densities.
    - **WEAVE + GUARANTEE + MOTIF MIX, 2026-08-08 (user: "weave these into each other and secure that something other than the main living core spawns... break up the blueprints into parts to mix and match"):** (a) **guarantee ladder** — secondary rolls (sleep 45 / comb 60 / ranks 30) now fall back to a forced comb→ranks→sleep attempt when all miss: every full camp gets ≥1 structure beyond the living core (22/28; the rest are honest thin-band core-onlys or waystops); at exactly one structure, 50% reaches for a SECOND. (b) **territorial fix** — the placers were starving each other (identical seam offsets; 0 multi-structure camps in 28): comb spots extended deeper both flanks, ranks tries both flanks → **7/28 multi-structure camps** incl. double-combs. (c) **weave pass** — structures link to their neighbors by extra spines (≤26 apart, 75%, max 2), the star becomes a cyclic network (18/28 camps); spines now REFUSE kept walls (posts/islands/rank-cores registered) so a blocked weave dead-ends honestly instead of gutting a room. (d) **motif mix** — carveComb's parts (rooms/wraps/teeth/stubs) are no longer all-rolled-always: each comb draws 2-4 parts from a weighted menu and logs them (`[rooms+wraps]`, `[stubs+teeth]`...) — the user's blueprint-parts framing made structural; districts within one camp differ in character. Verified 30 zones: 0 sealed throughout.
    - **SEALED-DONUT BUGFIX, 2026-08-08 (user reroll verdict #1: "lone disconnected 3x3/3x4 rooms with a pillar in the middle"):** the wrap move never carved a connector back to the walk — rings floated sealed unless the walk happened to brush them. Fix: wraps must find a DOOR CANDIDATE (ring cell orthogonally adjacent to walk-visited lattice, outside the skip arc) BEFORE carving — no candidate, no wrap — and the connector carves after the ring. Wrap rate 11→4 per ~20 combs (strict but every one reachable). **Plus a permanent ORPHAN TRIPWIRE:** the shell pass runs a fresh post-dress flood from the mouth and counts unreachable camp cells per camp (first version reused the pre-dress flood and false-flagged every alcove). Verified 30 zones: 28/28 camps at 0 orphans, 0 sealed. This class of bug can no longer ship silently.
    - **DEEP PASS + BACK-PASSAGES, 2026-08-08 (user reroll verdicts #2-3: "very few quarry sites are interconnected... huge swathes of plain stone where we could be doing a bit more"):** root cause — everything carved clung to the corridor's skin. (a) **Deep pass** (new genZone stage): 2-4 structures planted in genuinely deep mass (no open floor within 3) — pocket 35 / small comb 30 / deep gallery 20 / lone cell 15 — each tunneled to the nearest daylight (spiral search ≤22) and 80% chained to its neighbor: shafts into darkness, the DF-mine read. Deep combs may carve unattached — the shaft is their door. (b) **Back-passages**: sprawl records its placed workings (locality-sorted); consecutive pairs ≤12 apart link behind the face at 40% — quarry sites form districts. Verified 25 zones: 66 deep sites (~2.6/zone), 18 chains, 10 back-passages, 0 orphans, 0 sealed.
    - **THE DIGGER, 2026-08-08 (user: "is this a good case for the drillbot to actually work for construction? some generations that just had one making patterns"):** agent-based construction — one simulated digger whose TRACE is the generation. (Clarification banked: Qud's Drillbot is only a pathfinder movement profile; this is the stronger behavioral-agent idea, and in-game it's just a loop in the builder — no creature, no AI ticks.) Behavior: momentum-4/curl-2/anti-1 walk preferring fresh rock (35% hesitation re-walking own dig), action rolls — 3x3 lump-socket 6% (the quarrier rhyme), island room 3%, rest pocket 2%, branch-and-return 4% (stack ≤4), turn-around on stall (≤6), respects kept walls + protected shells. Budget 90-150 actions. **Connectivity guaranteed BY CONSTRUCTION — the orphan class cannot occur in a trace.** Standalone Digger button + 30% of zones get one. First stall bug (9-step runs: couldn't re-walk own tunnel, died in first corner) fixed same session. Traces run 76-114 steps, 1-4 sockets, 2-6 branches. Dials noted: room/pocket rates are conservative — tune on verdicts.
    - **SMALL-AND-WINDING RULING, 2026-08-08 (user: "deprioritize large rooms and just figure out how to hone our weird winding hallways and smallish rooms"):** (a) gallery fit-ladder flipped smallest-first, **9x7 grand gallery CUT** — 5x5 colonnade is now the default working, 7x5 the rare big one; (b) comb rooms bias 2x2-dominant while camp lattices LENGTHEN (5-7) — more hall per comb, less chamber; (c) **`windingTunnel` primitive** (noise-cost cardinal Dijkstra, kept-walls near-impassable, shells discouraged-but-doorable) replaces straight L-spines in ALL connective tissue — weave links, deep-site shafts, deep chains, sprawl back-passages. **The camp's main spine stays mason-straight deliberately: the two-voice contrast (chalk-line vs wander) is the ruled design.** Verified 20 zones: 0 orphans, 0 sealed, ~83ms/zone.
    - **FRESH-SLATE RESET, 2026-08-08 (user ruling: "the initial language that's baked in is kind of poisoning what we're trying now — generate it on a fresh blank slate, then try generating it with canyons, then generate it painted [= decorated, has items]"):** the legacy camp chain (mouth→home→spine→gallery→face, the session's oldest code) is recognized as an inherited skeleton every zone still wears under the patches. **Three-stage rebuild plan: (1) BLANK SLATE — the honed language composing alone, DELIVERED as `growWarren`/Warren button:** network growth with no chain — ports (frontier cells), winding hall from a port, small part rolled at its end (room 22 / mini-wrap 16 / ring unit 14 / mini comb 14 / pocket 14 / socket pair 10 / honest dead-end 10), 18% loop-backs between ports, 12-18 parts per warren. First rolls: 34-45 halls, 4-9 loops, full part spread — reads as the user's drawings 1-2 directly. **(2) IN CANYON — same generator constrained by real bands/mouths (next sitting, after stage-1 verdicts). (3) PAINTED — dressed with items (the deferred phase-2 tables).** The old Zone pipeline stays intact for comparison; it is now the LEGACY track. Verdict cycle: reroll Warren, judge the pure language, tune, then advance stages.
    - **CALIBRATION ROUNDS 7-8, 2026-08-08 (user's eighth + ninth drawings, "two more examples to refine with") — first rounds tuned against the FRESH warren:** drawing 8 gave the local grammar — **hooks** (G-spiral stubs curling around a wall pocket, 5% part roll) and **footed dead-ends** (stubs that turn once at the tip, replacing flat dead-ends). Drawing 9 gave a second HALL VOICE — **trunks** (30% of halls): long (12-24), mostly straight with sparse 15% single jogs — the staircase-step hand, purposeful travel vs the local wander; trunk convergence produces drawing 9's central-knot structure emergently. Verified: all features fire (5-16 trunks, 1-4 hooks, 0-3 feet, 0-11 loops per warren). Corpus now nine drawings; the warren speaks both the near hand and the far hand.
    - **NO-TUNNEL-WITHOUT-A-REASON, 2026-08-08 (user verdict: "warren still generates a lot of empty, long spaces rather than tunnels-into-rooms — but I might be diluting the thing that makes it feel like a stonecarver's quarry"):** the design answer, banked — **tunnels-into-rooms is dungeon logic; no-tunnel-without-a-reason is quarry logic.** A dungeon's halls deliver destinations; a quarry's halls exist because every foot cost labor — the threat to quarry-feel is purposelessness and REGULARITY of payoff, not rooms per se. Built: (a) **every hall ends in work** — failed part rolls fall back room→socket→prospector's-foot (an abandoned test-dig is also work); (b) **trunks are haulage-ways** — 12%/step side-bites along their length (loading bays), length trimmed 12-24→10-20; (c) local halls trimmed 4-10→4-8. Effect: halls-to-parts ratio collapsed ~3:1 → ~1:1 (12-17 halls now yield 15-17 parts vs 34-45 halls for the same parts before) — the bare-hall bloat is gone while payoff KIND stays irregular (chamber / sockets / feet). The quarry-ness lives in work-evidence + extraction causality + fit-rule irregularity, all preserved.
    - **CALIBRATION ROUND 9, 2026-08-08 (user's tenth drawing, "constrained rooms"): the SUITE** — a bounded footprint (7-10 × 5-7, strict all-wall validation) whose interior is subdivided into 2-4 small rooms by shared 1-cell partitions, doors punched per partition, ONE entrance from the arriving hall. The motif the warren lacked: rooms PACKED against each other (domestic architecture behind a door) vs the isolated dilations everywhere else. 14% part roll (part table rebalanced: room 16 / suite 14 / wrap 14 / unit 12 / comb 10 / pocket 12 / sockets 10 / hook 6 / foot 6); fires in ~1/3 of warrens (footprint is demanding — fits where the network left space). Dial noted: feet counts run high (7-10/warren — the work-guarantee ladder lands on prospector's-feet often in dense networks); tune if the user reads it as stubby. Corpus: ten drawings.
    - **PROTECTED SHELL added same session (user: "central areas spawn with a third of their walls gone"):** root cause — pocket walls validated at carve time but eaten afterward: sprawl bites tolerate 15-30% open footprint (can punch through a 1-cell pocket wall), and the pinch police would happily OPEN a diagonal pinch between pocket interior and corridor. Fix: genQuarry collects every camp-chain cell (home/threshold/spine/gallery/face/alcoves/comb/bore/digs/back-door), marks interiors `protectedFloor` + all adjacent walls `protectedShell` (8-dir, ~70-150 walls/camp); sprawl bites reject any footprint touching either; police never clears shell walls. Verified 30 zones: 29 camps, 0 sealed, pockets stay interior.
    - **WARREN LAB BUILT 2026-08-11 — `design/warren-lab.html`, the fresh-slate track's dedicated rig** (standalone carve-focused fork of tile-sketch; own localStorage keys `tfwarren*`, tile-sketch canvases untouched). What it adds over tile-sketch: **46 knobs in 7 groups** (every formerly-hardcoded generator number; live reroll, "Reset all" = the 2026-08-08 tuned baseline verified byte-identical, "Copy diff" emits only changed knobs; part-table weights are real weights now, not a cumulative R(1,100) if-chain); **roll history in its own list** that brush strokes never truncate (fixes the v1 lost-roll bug), each entry storing seed AND the knob values it rolled under — a landed roll is reproducible, not just a picture; **vocabulary gallery** — 23 cards, one per primitive, each rendered by the generator's OWN carve function on a scratch grid (cards cannot drift from code), role-tag filters, per-card local persistent reference-image slot; **Qud tiles toggle** — walls masked off neighbours exactly as `ZoneManager.PaintWall` does, floors rolling `sw_ground_dots` variants, kept walls (standing cores / phase-2 slots) amber-tinted. Legacy camp chain kept behind a collapsed disclosure. Sprites live in gitignored `design/tiles/walls/` (Freehold's assets — the repo carries `extract-qud-walls.py`, the recipe, not the art; lab degrades gracefully if the folder is empty). Extraction path documented in the script: regenerate Unity type trees from `Assembly-CSharp.dll` via UnityPy's TypeTreeGenerator, deserialize the ~27k `exTextureInfo` index, crop from the `Kobold_DynamicAtlas_Walls*` pages; tint baked in (file:// canvas taint), `--body/--detail/--swap` to reskin. **UNVERIFIED Claude inference: black=body(TileColor) / white=detail(DetailColor)** — chosen because black is ~78% of pixels and shale should read mostly red; not confirmed in the shader; `--swap` if inverted vs the real game.
    - **GROUND TRUTH CORRECTED 2026-08-11 (re-decompiled against the current Steam install — decompiled fact, not proposal). These change generated shape:** (1) **the Drillbot's noise is not a smooth cost field** — `AddNoise` builds `NoiseMap(80,25,10, 3,3, 4, 80,80, 6, 3,-3, 1)` then sets weight = FLAT 98 for every cell whose noise exceeds 1 (~76% of the zone), sub-cutoff cells keeping terrain weight (solid wall = real obstacles); a canyon is a shortest path through ~21% blob obstacles — long clean runs bending hard around blobs, NOT the fine-grained wobble of the 2026-08-07 reconstruction's `1 + white*6`. (2) **Stairs count as connections** — CanyonBuilder paths to every Stairs connection as well as every Canyon one, and those endpoints are interior: real canyons grow spurs to mid-zone staircases; the sim never modelled this. (3) **The 4×4 hub clearing happens only on the Start branch**, and with a Canyon Start connection the hub sits AT that connection (an edge cell), not at a random interior point. (4) **`GetLocalAdjacentCells(r)` is a filled Chebyshev box clamped to 0..W-1/0..H-1** — widening reaches the zone edge, it does not stop at the border ring. (5) `Stat.Random(1, high)` rolled once per path cell: confirmed unchanged. (6) **`PaintWall` mask order is N NE E SE S SW W NW**, off-map neighbours read 0 via `bEdgeFlag`; a neighbour connects on same-blueprint, same `PaintWith`, `PaintWith == "*"`, or having the `PaintPart` part (same convention as `Fx_StoneRise.ComposeTile`/`ISOLATED_MASK`). (7) **All 256 `wall_rock` masks are stocked** (counted in the tile index) — no carve shape can hit the blank-`InvalidInfo` path. (8) **Village hut floors are `DirtPath` on every interior cell**, hardcoded in `VillageMaker` AND `VillageBase` for every faction — goatfolk identity is `ThatchedWall` + `RoundBuildings` + population, not floors; `DirtPath` inherits `DirtFloor` with `RandomTileOnMove` over four `sw_ground_dots` variants. **Qud's own idiom: walls autotile off neighbours, floors roll one of N** — governs any floor work we do.
    - **THREE BUGS FOUND + FIXED 2026-08-11 — two were corrupting the design record:** (1) **the mini comb had NEVER fired in a warren** — `carveComb` demands an all-wall footprint but the call site centred it on the cell the hall had just carved, so the check failed on the hall itself every time (20 warrens with comb as the ONLY table part: zero combs). Now placed ahead of the hall end like the suite; fires ~1 warren in 3. ~~The same bug is still LIVE in `tile-sketch.html` (identical call, line ~1208) — port-pending.~~ **PORTED TO tile-sketch 2026-08-11 (vine sitting): both the comb call-site fix AND the ranks attach-flag revert (the second was also live there — growWarren originated in tile-sketch, so it carried both). Verified in-browser: 21 rolls, combs land in 6 (~1 in 3.5, was zero-possible), unattached combs/units carve-then-revert in the log.** (2) **66% of rank units were sealed** — `carveRanks` reports whether its lane reached open floor and `growWarren` ignored the flag (15 unit-only warrens: 57 of 86 accepted units were ones carveRanks itself had logged UNATTACHED). Now reverts like the comb; units drop ~6/warren → ~1. **Any ranks judgement made before 2026-08-11 was made on two-thirds-broken output.** (3) gallery ring-unit demo card drew its lane and depth axes parallel (a bar, not a unit) — gallery-only, no generator impact.
    - **DIAGONAL SEAL — USER RULING 2026-08-11 (vine sitting, judging a positive-example warren roll; the flag: a room enterable through a corner-kiss with the hall — "there's a diagonal section where you can walk into the room - but if we just fill that from inside the room, then you can still walk down the hallway without even noticing it was there in the first place"). BUILT into warren-lab same sitting:** Qud walks diagonals, so a chamber corner diagonally touching outside floor IS a door nobody dug; the rule seals it from the CHAMBER side — the hall keeps its line, the room goes quiet behind its real entrance (discovery, not announcement — rooms you walk past without knowing). Implementation: `sealChamberDiagonals` — chamber-family parts (room / suite / wrap / ring unit / comb / pocket + fallback rooms) seal at birth against pre-existing floor, then one final union sweep catches halls carved past older rooms; doorway guard (a cell orthogonally open to outside floor is never sealed), and every fill runs an 8-dir connectivity flood (Qud movement rules) reverting any fill that would strand a cell — a room whose ONLY entrance was the leak keeps the leak. Work-bites (sockets/hooks/feet) stay open to the hall by design. **Inversion note: the legacy grammar police OPENS diagonal pinches (Ruiner idiom) — that law still governs the legacy track; the seal is warren-only.** New knob `sealDiag` (warren — grammar group, default ON) for A/B against old behavior. Verified 30 rolls: seals fire in ~30% of warrens (8 rolls ×1, 1 roll ×2), 0 stranded cells (independent flood check on every export), 0 errors. Port-pending to tile-sketch + eventual ZonePainter per the sim-first workflow.
    - **HIDDEN GEOLOGY / WET POCKETS — USER CONCEPT 2026-08-11 (same camp jam, answering Claude's "no geology events" critique of a warren roll): "3x3 squares (or really any shape) full of pools of salty water, but all the walls around it secure - so a player wouldn't see the pools directly (cave walls are high), but they could see what surrounds it - cave moss, small flora, muddy soil, leaks from the wall, puddles." Irregular-interior refinement same sitting ("the avoidance pockets, internally, do not need to be a perfect square"). BUILT into warren-lab same sitting, knobs in a new "warren — geology" group:** one sealed brine pocket per warren at `wetChance` (def 0.45 — iteration-friendly, final rarity is a USER DIAL), size 2-3 per axis with 40% corner drops (never gutted below 3 cells), anchored 6-14 cells off the warren start so the network grows toward it; pool + 1-ring shell registered protected across every carve path (comb/blob/wrap gained explicit guards — latent holes anyway), tunnels refuse pool/shell at hard cost, and a soft `wetAvoid` ring (CLAUDE dial, def on at 8) makes halls conspicuously bend around the wet ground — the avoided-mass read. Symptoms are dress-phase (`gendress` only; bare stage-1 geometry shows just the sealed water + the bend): distance-gradient halo — full kit pressed near the shell (moss/seep/mud/≤2 puddles), thinning to stray mud at reach (`wetHalo`), one guaranteed tell if the rolls all miss (a symptomless pocket is indistinguishable from rock). New palette terrain: muddy soil `;` + cave moss `%` (seep reuses the existing trough object). **Verified 40 dressed rolls: 13 pockets, 13/13 sealed (blob-level breach check on every export), 13/13 irregular, 13/13 symptomatic, 0 disconnects.** Watch item: one 1/30 disconnect flag in an early run, unreproduced across 100 subsequent checked rolls — likely checker artifact. **Convergences noted at the sitting (Claude, for design leverage): Geomancy's reveal-through-stone is the natural prospecting verb for hidden pockets · wet moss = the moss-boxes supply chain (morestonecarver.txt float) · brine pools = what wild pig vessels graze back to (the re-dilution behavior the boar ruling wrote) · SALTY is the economy guard (fresh water in walls would be currency) · pickaxe players CAN breach — symptoms as treasure map, mild reward by construction. OPEN, user's to rule: final rarity · a rare pre-breached variant (flooded working, the one time they didn't avoid it — CLAUDE float) · symptom flora specifics at the C# port (base blueprint picks).** Port-pending with the seal.
      - **MOSS = WALL-MOUNTED, RULED same sitting (user: "i think the rock should spawn on the wall? that way we can just cheaply recolor it when it's time to harvest? and then just have an item for the harvested bit" · "can we just recolor the wall tile directly if it has it?").** Floor-moss tile CUT from the palette (existed ~minutes); sim now marks `mossyWalls` on open-faced walls in the halo gradient and recolors the ACTUAL wall sprite — no overlay, mirroring the game shape. **RECOLOR THROUGH THE QUD PALETTE (user direction, same sitting: "just add support for these colors in our tester" + color picks "tile color could be #155352 detail #009403 maybe? so it's greyish with a dark green highlight" — those hexes ARE the engine's `K` and `g`, so the blueprint says `TileColor="&K" DetailColor="g"`):** warren-lab gained the 16-code `QUDCOLORS` table (K/g corroborated by the user's hexes; rest = the standard modding-wiki palette, spot-check any code before shipping art on it) + `ensureQudFilter` (SVG luminance-threshold discrete-transfer filter — maps the baked two-tone sprite to EXACT target hexes per (tile, detail) pair, no canvas taint) + `mossTile`/`mossDetail` knob dropdowns (geology group, string-valued sel via a new `str` knob flag) so the moss look iterates live in palette-legal codes only. User's K/g marked "maybe" — provisional pick, the knobs exist to settle it. **XML-ONLY VERDICT (base-XML recon same sitting): the whole game-side behavior is attribute-driven on the stock `Harvestable` part** — `OnSuccess`/`OnSuccessAmount` (the moss item), `DestroyOnHarvest="false"` (wall stays; Lagroot precedent, Creatures.xml:6175), `Ripe*/Unripe*` color triplets (**the cheap recolor is native — one blueprint, two color states**), custom `HarvestVerb`. Not plant-bound: banana trees (ZoneTerrain.xml:91) + furniture (Furniture.xml:2242) carry it, and etching already proved wall twiddle menus. Build = mossy-wall blueprint + moss item blueprint, ZERO new C#; placement rides the wet-pocket painter port. Caveats on file: harvest is gated by the Harvestry skill (design consequence to accept or route around) · re-ripen-after-harvest timer = one decomp check at build. Moss item doubles as the moss-boxes supply (edible, slight taste effect — the morestonecarver float).
    - **STAGE 2 (WARREN IN CANYON) — ATTACHMENT RULES, proposal sitting 2026-08-11. Design only, nothing built yet.** Core frame (CLAUDE PROPOSAL, accepted by the rulings below): `growWarren` learns nothing about canyons per se — canyon floor becomes its own cell class, and the one new law is halls REFUSE canyon floor except at deliberate mouths (the wet-pocket/protected-shell guard shape, third use); the thin-band fold is expected to emerge from refusal alone (corridor forbidden + zone edge = only the seam has mass), so NO band-steering gets pre-engineered — lay a faithful canyon, mask it, one mouth, roll twenty, judge first. Forks ruled at the sitting: **(1) RULED (user: "agree with this lean") — one mouth per warren + ~30% back door**, carrying over the articulation batch's lived-in-burrows-have-two-exits; multiple free ports rejected for trading away the discovery quality the diagonal seal protects. **(2) RULED (user: "this sounds fine") — a warren never crosses the corridor**; a band is one warren's territory, both-bands-inhabited = two warrens. **(3) RULED (user: "agreed") — wet pockets and deep sites FAIL HONESTLY in bands too thin for their depth requirements** (pocket anchor 6-14, deep-pass isolation 3), spawn-nothing-fallback spirit; no shrink-to-fit (a pocket without room for its halo is a puddle). **(4) RULED same sitting (user counter-direction "we can integrate these spurs maybe?" → Claude integration proposal → user: "i like the shape of that ruling") — SPURS AS ATTACHMENT SURFACE:** mouth candidates = wall skin of the whole canyon system (corridor + spurs); spur floor stays refused to non-mouth halls (integration = attachment, not exception — the breach law survives as one law over a bigger surface); mild spur-mouth preference (~2:1 per cell, CLAUDE DIAL — enough to read as a habit, corridor mouths stay unsurprising) for the pilgrim-road fiction (lore ruling: drawn to deep caves — the staircase is the draw). Accepted consequences, riding unruled: stairs unblockable BY CONSTRUCTION (refusal never removes connectivity, mouths only add) · spur-mouthed warren + ~30% corridor back door = emergent through-warren shortcut between corridor and staircase (user saw it flagged, shape accepted — revisit rarity only if play says so) · staircase wraps emerge from seam-folding, no special code. Standing note: `CanyonMarker` survival stays unverified but gates only the eventual ZonePainter port — the lab generates its own canyon. **BUILT + VERIFIED same day (warren-lab lab pass; user: "let's build our lab pass"):** new **Canyon Warren** button (`genCanyonWarren`) composing the faithful canyon gen + `growWarren` untouched — canyon floor becomes a masked cell class, every wall 8-touching it (Qud walks diagonals) is SKIN registered in `keepWalls`+`protectedShell` (the wet-shell idiom's third use — every carver refuses it for free); mouth picker = skin cells with walkable non-water canyon face + `mouthDepth` (def 3) solid rock behind, spur-facing candidates weighted `spurPull` (def 2); `windingTunnel` gains hard canyon refusal PLUS a pre-carve crossing belt (a Dijkstra path forced across the canyon carves NOTHING instead of stranding a fragment on the far side); back door per the ruling (`backDoor` def 0.30, `backDoorMin` def 10, sites = skin between warren floor and non-water canyon floor); wet pockets keep stage-1 anchoring with a new same-band guard (cross-corridor anchors rejected — a pocket the warren can't approach is god-view-only); knobs in a new "warren — canyon (stage 2)" group; both openings render as `>`. Two permanent TRIPWIRES per roll: breach count over remaining skin (expect 0) and warren-side reach (flood from mouth refusing canyon floor must hit every warren cell). **Verified 30 rolls: 30/30 clean — 0 breaches, 0 disconnects, 0 JS errors; spur mouths 11/30 at pull 2; parts avg 14 (med 15, min 5 — thin-band squeezes are honest, not failures); wet 8 placed / 2 honest no-fit fails. Showcase read (seed 200, ascii export): spur mouth → halls fold down the band around the sealed pool → back door beside the staircase slot — the pilgrim through-route EMERGED from the two ruled laws touching, zero steering code.** Two stage-1 fixes found en route: **(1) `carveWrap` carved its DOOR cell unguarded** — a ring that validated clean could still punch its door through a protected shell (wet-pocket or canyon skin); door cell now validated. **Same hole is presumably live in `tile-sketch.html` (growWarren's origin) — port-pending with the seal.** (2) **`sealChamberDiagonals`'s strand check rewritten global→relative** (`allOpenReachable` → before/after `reachCount` comparison): the old law compared against ALL open cells, so a canyon marsh's legitimately-isolated CA ponds would have silently reverted every seal in the zone; the honest question is whether THIS fill stranded anything. Dials flagged for user judgment, all CLAUDE numbers: back door lands ~3/11 rolled (legal sites ≥10 from the mouth are scarce in thin bands — `backDoorMin` is the dial if more through-routes wanted) · feet still run high (avg 8.1, the stage-1 flag unchanged) · sub-10-part rolls in skinny bands (spawn-nothing-adjacent; judge whether a 5-part warren reads as a hermit hole or noise). **VERDICT ROUND 1 same day (user, three screenshot reads: ex1 "warren is too small" · ex2 "warren has too much empty space" · ex3 "decent size and shape warren, but a bit too early to tell") — diagnosis: the ATTEMPT + PAYOFF ECONOMY still assumed a blank slate. Fixes, all gated on canyon presence so stage-1 keeps its exact rng sequence (drawing calibration NOT re-rolled; sanity batch confirms stage-1 numbers byte-identical):** (a) attempt economy — blocked trunks fall through to a local hall in the same attempt, hall targeting re-rolls direction/length ≤4x (doomed targets no longer burn whole attempts); (b) payoff economy — the ROLLED part tries all four rotations before the fallback ladder, and the fallback chamber tries rotations before degrading to a foot (chamber carvers only ever faced the hall's own direction — facing skin, they failed into feet). Measured 101-130 before→after: parts min 5→12 (avg 14→15.1, always in the wanted 12-18 band now — ex1's mechanism dead), chambers min 0→~1 (avg 4→4.5 vs stage-1's 5.4), feet avg 9.5→8.5 (vs stage-1 6.2 — remaining gap is honest space reality, dial if it still reads stubby). Phantom loop count fixed in passing (crossing-belt empty paths no longer logged as loops). **PLUS A TRIPWIRE CATCH, the guard system paying for itself: seed 117 rolled 2 BREACHES — the comb and ranks ATTACH PROBES carve their connector UNGUARDED (no keepWalls/protectedShell check on intermediates, any non-wall accepted as daylight): through canyon skin to canyon floor, and — LATENT IN STAGE 1 — through the wet shell to pool WATER (a probe hitting brine reads it as open floor and unseals the pocket), and through kept posts/rank cores the shard police protects. Both probes fixed (guarded wall = not this way; canyon/pool floor = not daylight); 60-roll re-verify 60/60 clean incl. seed 117. The probe holes are presumably LIVE IN tile-sketch.html (both carvers exist there) — port-pending with the seal + the carveWrap door guard.** Stage-2 re-judge queued: user re-rolls against the fixed economy (ex3's "too early to tell" stands as the open verdict).
    - **MOUTH MASS WEIGHTING + TRIPWIRE CATCH #2, 2026-08-12 (user anti-example verdict while judging a stage-2 batch: "the cave is going to the sparsest section instead of the full areas below" — seed 319, a thin-band warren over a huge untouched mass).** Diagnosis: the mouth picker's only mass check was `mouthDepth` (3 solid cells straight behind, pass/fail); past that every skin cell weighed 1 (×spurPull), and a long thin band offers MORE skin cells than a compact deep mass — the picker was mildly biased toward exactly the wrong rock, and thin-band mouths are foot factories (319 rolled 10 feet of 14 parts). **RULED (user: "let's try that first fix"): mouth candidates are now weighted by the carvable mass behind them** — BFS through wall only (skin refused, same as every carver) within Chebyshev `mouthMassR` (new stage-2 knob, def 7; 0 = flat pre-2026-08-12 weights), weight = mass × spurPull. Verified 30 rolls: chosen mouths average 72% of the zone's best candidate mass, 28/30 in the top half; all-thin zones still produce honest thin-band warrens (weight, not filter — the hermit question stays open). **TRIPWIRE CATCH #2 en route (re-aimed mouths made seeds 304/311 trip the reach wire — bug EXPOSED by the change, latent since the wet-pocket build):** `windingTunnel`'s crossing belt only checked canyon cells, but Dijkstra pays ANY 4000 toll when that's the only route — a path could THREAD THE WET POCKET'S KEPT SHELL (or pool water), the carve step silently skips kept walls, and the tunnel opens on both sides of an intact wall: a sealed fragment with ZERO breaches (seed 304: the mouth pocket was hemmed by the pool, so all 12 halls paid the shell toll and the entire warren grew as an unreachable fragment north of the pool, reach 6/24, pool still sealed). Fixes, both the belt idiom: (a) the belt now rejects paths threading kept walls / pool water / wet shell — such a path carves NOTHING and the attempt economy retries elsewhere; (b) sibling hole in `carveTrunk`: its walk broke only on protected WALLS, so open canyon floor (reachable via the mouth cell, which is unregistered) and pool water never stopped it — a trunk could stroll across the corridor and resume carving on the far side; the walk now breaks at any cell it may neither carve nor claim. Re-verified: stage-2 30/30 clean incl. 304/311 (304 now collapses honestly to a 4-part squeeze — its pool-hemmed mouth pocket really is that small); stage-1 20 rolls, 0 errors, feet avg 6.2→5.8 (the drift = ex-buggy pool-wading trunks now break early; those rolls were broken output, not calibration). **Port-pending to tile-sketch with the rest of the seal batch: the threading hole is presumably LIVE there** (windingTunnel + trunks exist; kept posts/rank cores are threadable the same way; no wet pockets to worry about). Dials flagged (CLAUDE observations): 304-class rolls (a pool eating a cramped mouth's growing room) are the hermit-vs-noise question wearing a new hat · spurPull ×2 can still outvote mass toward a poor spur mouth (seed 304 chose 45-of-127 mass) — dial if it recurs · the foot-heavy fallback ladder remains the OTHER half of the feet problem, unruled (stage-2 feet avg 8.5 after this fix; the mass weighting attacked placement, not fallback).
      - **CLASS-AWARE MOUTH FITTING — the predicted recurrence arrived and got its fix, RULED 2026-08-12 (user verdict on seed 16: "this current seed has a *lot* of negative space" — diagnosis: the weighted mouth lottery drew a mass-16 pocket with 169 on offer, starving a medium warren into a 90-cell scribble with 8-of-15 feet, wet pocket AND gallery both honest-failing in the sliver; Claude offered relative-mass-floor vs class-aware placement, user: "i'll go with your lean").** Build: the size class rolls BEFORE the mouth (extracted `rollWarrenClass`/`classFromBand`; growWarren takes an optional pre-rolled class, stage-1 unchanged), a mouth is eligible for the class only if `mass >= partsWanted x mouthMassPerPart` (new knob, def 7 — CLAUDE dial, the observed rock-consumed-per-part footprint), and when no candidate feeds the class it DEGRADES large → medium → small before anything carves — the rock decides what fits; a small warren in a sliver is a hermit hole, a medium one is a bug. Last resort (all candidates below even small's need): the best pocket digs anyway as a cramped hermit dig — spawn-nothing stays reserved for zero candidates (CLAUDE call). `mouthMassR = 0` disables fitting with the weighting, preserving the legacy A/B. **Verified 30 rolls: 30/30 tripwires clean, degradation fired 10/30 (thin bands stepping down honestly), 0 cramped-dig fallbacks, avg chosen mouth 84% of zone-best mass (was 72% under pure weighting; the seed-16 failure mode was 9%).
