    - **RIVER/MARSH DECOMP-VERIFIED + TRIPWIRE CATCH #3, 2026-08-12 (user, judging a marsh roll: "is water generation broken or is this normal?" → ruling "let's make sure river is accurate to base game decomp").** The river layer was the last piece of the canyon still running on the 2026-08-07 reconstruction (its own code comment said so). Re-decompiled `RiverBuilder` + `Genkit.CellularGrid` via `ilspycmd` against the Steam install (recipe: `ilspycmd -t <TypeName> ".../Caves of Qud/CoQ_Data/Managed/Assembly-CSharp.dll"` — same dll the sprite extractor uses). **Verdict on the user's screenshot: the zone-wide pond splat is FAITHFUL** — marsh CA really does cover the whole zone, enclosed-in-rock ponds included (in game they'd be invisible behind high walls; the lab's god-view exaggerates). **Five reconstruction drifts found and fixed in the sim:** (1) CA rule was born≥5/survive≥4, real is **born {6,7,8} / survive {5,6,7,8}** — the game's marsh is sparser and blobbier than the sim's was; (2) real CA forces the border ring DEAD every pass (SeedBorders=false), sim let seeded edges live; (3) real grid is **80x30 with only rows 1..23 stamped** (bottom rows are live neighbors, not a border); (4) **the lone-mouth marsh zone still gets its river channel** — pathed from a random interior point (5-75, 5-20) out to the lone mouth, the river rising out of the marsh; the sim had marsh replace the river entirely; (5) channel cost is `|simplex(x/3,y)|*190 + 20/wall` — the abs-fold makes rivers follow noise valleys, x stretched 3:1; sim now folds its value noise to match (value noise itself stays a stand-in for FastNoise simplex). Marsh mechanism confirmed: fires iff the zone has EXACTLY ONE river connection (river dead-ends here — world-map topology); `riverChance`/`marshChance` knobs stay as stand-ins for that topology, help text updated. Also confirmed: marsh = `ClearWalls` + `SaltyWaterDeepPool` per cell (salty ✓ the economy guard), `Cell.ClearTerrain` DOES remove walls (channel cuts rock), and RiverBuilder contains a delightful dead line spawning a Drillbot creature into the void when BuildTries>5. Verified 100 stage-2 rolls: marsh 8/100, ponds now 1-3 cohesive components (70-150 water cells) channel-linked to a mouth instead of confetti. **TRIPWIRE CATCH #3 en route (seed 464, exposed by the river resequencing rng): a hall pathed ALONG THE ZONE BORDER ROW** — `noisyPath` must allow the border (canyon mouths live there), but every carver's `inB` and the reach tripwire exclude it, so a hall routed via y=H-1 carved legally yet sat under the flood's floor: 8 "stranded" cells that were really connected through the ring. Fix, belt idiom again: border ring priced 4000 in `windingTunnel`'s cost AND belt-rejected — a hall that can only reach its target along the zone seam carves nothing. The reach tripwire also gained a stranded-cell coordinate dump on failure (hunting this took three tracers; the next hunt starts with coordinates for free). Re-verified: **stage-2 100/100 clean, feet avg 8.5→7.8** (border-crawler halls were junk miles), stage-1 20 rolls 0 errors. Port-pending: the border-ring hole exists wherever tile-sketch's windingTunnel runs.
    - **STAGE 3 FIRST BITE — CANYON FLORA (grass + trees), USER REQUEST 2026-08-12 ("warren v3 having grass and trees painted like the canyon has in game"). GROUND TRUTH PULLED + BUILT INTO warren-lab SAME DAY.** How the game actually does it, all decomp/XML-read today (ilspycmd + StreamingAssets): **(1) the grass is floor PAINT, not objects** — every surface zone's population drops a `Dirty` FloorPainter widget (Widgets.xml:142; `DesertCanyonZoneGlobals` carries its own `<object Blueprint="Dirty"/>` row at PopulationTables.xml:6418) whose `Dirty.PaintCell` runs over EVERY cell in the zone, walls included: d8 → 1-5 brown dirt (`&w`, `Tiles/tile-dirt1.png`, detail `k`) / 6 dark grass `&g` / 7 bright grass `&G` / 8 pale dirt `&y`; grass rolls one of `DirtPicker`'s four tiles (`tile-grass1` + `Terrain/sw_grass1-3`); text mode rolls d5 over `. , ` '` / nothing. Grass never sets a detail color — it lands on `Cell.Render`'s explored-cell default `"k"`, so detail is k across the board (decomp-confirmed, not a guess). **Consequence worth eyes: rock carved open later shows the paint that was always underneath — in game the WARREN floor will grow the same dirt-and-grass, buried greenery included.** The sim shows this faithfully (paint covers all floor); if buried grass reads wrong in play, the C# painter can repaint warren interiors — USER CALL at port time, nothing pre-engineered. **(2) the trees are population objects** — `ZoneTemplate:DesertCanyon` global table `DesertCanyonZoneGlobals` (PopulationTables.xml:6416): Dogthorn Chance 95 Number 30-80, Witchwood 25/5-20, Starapple 5/5-20, scattered on any empty cell; the template is a POSTBUILDER running after carve + river and before any mod post-builder, so trees stand on canyon floor only and the warren (carved after) never gets any — the sim scatters at the canyon generator's tail, exactly that slot. `[verify]` at C# port time: our painter's actual position in the postbuilder order. **Built:** new "canyon — flora (stage 3)" knob group (16 knobs, defaults ARE the game numbers: floor-look select with the old sw_ground_dots stand-in kept as legacy option · the d8 as four weights · glyph chance · chance/min/max per species); floor paint hashes off the ROLL's seed (`paintSeed`, persisted in autosave + history + kept canvases — no shimmer within a roll, fresh ground every reroll; the game rerolls per zone build); grass/dirt sprites extracted as black/white masks and recolored live through `ensureQudFilter` to exact palette codes (the moss path's second customer — zero baked tints); tree palette entries recolored to blueprint glyph truth (dogthorn `&G`, witchwood `&W`, starapple `&R`, ZoneTerrain.xml). `extract-qud-walls.py` gained manifest MERGE with per-tile tint records (a floor run no longer clobbers the walls manifest). **Verified in-browser (glyph mode — the pane's data: snapshot can't load sprite files; tile-mode eyes-on = next time the user opens the lab from disk, the filter path itself is the proven moss machinery):** canyon roll logs `flora: dogthorn 37, witchwood 0, starapple 0` (gates behaving), Canyon Warren end-to-end clean — 64 dogthorn placed BEFORE the skin scan, then mouth pick 22 candidates / 13 parts / 0 tripwire breaches / reach 142/142; 0 JS errors. CLAUDE dials on record: the d5 glyph roll compressed to one chance knob (0.8 = the real 4-in-5) · trees at the canyon tail also ride the legacy Zone chain (faithful — the template dresses those zones too). Sprites remain gitignored per the standing rule; the repo carries the recipe. **FIX ROUND same day (user first-look: "it's not loading tiles?" — tiles WERE loading; the recolor was eating them): CSS `filter` recolors the element's ENTIRE painted output, and the floor cells' near-black background + dark outline both sat below the luminance threshold → mapped to the body color → solid slabs reading as broken tiles. Moss never hit this because wall sprites are full-bleed opaque. Fix: Dirty-floor cells go transparent-background + transparent-outline so the filter only ever sees the mask (lesson comment left at the call site). Two more in the same pass: (1) atlas m_Names carry mixed case (`Tile-Dirt1`) — files renamed lowercase + the extractor now normalizes keys on save (Windows forgives the mismatch, nothing else would); (2) TREES UPGRADED to the game's own masks — the old bg_ pngs are baked in two LIGHT tones (y + detail), which the luminance threshold would flatten to one color, so the three species now extract their real sprites (`sw_fattree1/2`, `sw_talltree1/2`, `sw_starappletree`) and recolor live to blueprint TileColor/DetailColor (dogthorn G/w, witchwood W/g, starapple g/R), variant rolled by coord hash = the blueprints' own RandomTile pair; bg_ pngs stay as the no-tiles fallback. Verified over http this time (new `.claude/launch.json` "warren-lab" config serves design/ on :8123 — the pane's data: snapshot can't load relative sprites, http can): canyon roll renders dark ground + dirt speckle + two-green tufts + green dogthorns with brown trunks against red shale, 0 console errors.**

(2) Base Quartz Baboon overlap resolved by register split: quartz = stone SKIN (armor/reflect, L22 subterrane), gravel = stone THROWN (barrage/whorl, early canyon) — this is why the skin-flavored working name died.** Chassis: `Baboon` child (StoneScavenger merge + pocket stones inherited — he fetches his own spent barrage when charges lag). **COBBLEFUR FAMILY SETTLED + BUILT (2026-08-13, second sitting — the full three-piece butchery set, quartzfur idiom; supersedes the interim one-bracer plan): corpse chance 15 DRAFT on a custom corpse, pickone table at equal weights (base Quartz Baboon Corpse mirror). All three pieces: Fur + organic + `AddsRep Baboons -100` each (quartzfur precedent — the rep hit stacks per piece worn, self-balancing). Tiles = recolored base placeholders, sprite debt filed ×3.** **(1) Cobblefur Bracer** (arm; the 2026-08-04 baboon-skin throw bracers landed): `CloseThrowRangeAccuracyBonus` +25 DRAFT via `Part_CobblefurBracer.cs` — engine truth banked from decomp: within throw range the property scales aim scatter by (100−total)/100 AND skips the fall-short distance-variance branch; base's only granter is the Parabolic Muscular Subroutine (arm-slot cybernetic, +100 — our item is its low-tech ancestor in the same slot); engine takes max(item-line, skill-line), no skill double-dip; sources SUM within the line. **Cap-aware lend/return (USER CATCH: "chimeras can have potentially unlimited arms" — arm count is not a natural cap, and >100 flips the multiplier negative): equip adds min(Bonus, 100−current) and serializes the receipt, unequip returns exactly it; a limb-stacked chimera reaching exactly 100 through sheer dead monkey is the intended joke, paid in stacked rep.** Verified compose-safe with the streakschorl lend (its AimVariance=0 is direct; the bracer's property covers the fall-short skip the lend existed for). **(2) Cobblefur Gloves** (hands, AV0/DV1; the 2026-08-02 gearset catch gloves landed): flat uncontested 25% DRAFT to snatch an incoming THROWN rock (IsAnyStone filter) via `Part_CobblefurGloves.cs` — rides the whorl's own `DefenderMissileHitEvent` consume idiom, item-listens-on-wearer per the Yurtmat trick; caught rock auto-readies into an empty thrown slot (Claude lean built, the gearset's old fork — veto cheap), else inventory. **(3) Cobblefur Cloak** (back, AV1/DV0 user-ruled; sprite need user-flagged at concept): +2 DV DRAFT while amid loose rubble — **"rocks" RULED loose floor litter (user: "loose floor litter, rubble?"), adjacent-inclusive; chassis = base Yurtmat verbatim (user pointer: "check how the grassy yurtmat works") — StatShifter DV shift named "rubble cover," PlantProperties→IsAnyStone, same-tile widened to adjacent, plus an EndTurn re-check Yurtmat doesn't need (rubble ARRIVES around stationary fighters here — spent barrage, fissure fallout). The litter ecology as your cover; third body on the accumulation signature.** **ADJACENT SCOPE RE-EXAMINED AND CONFIRMED (user challenge + ruling 2026-08-13, "i agree with that thinking"): user asked if adjacent outmuscles Yurtmat's same-tile (their impression was Yurtmat = adjacent too; decomp says same-tile only). Kept adjacent on three legs: (1) solidity split — small stones/boulders standable, but `LargeBoulder` is Solid and base `Rubble` is a solid Walls.xml heap, so same-tile would make the most cover-shaped rocks the only ones that can't give cover; (2) same-tile would coach standing ON rocks, colliding with the discharge law; (3) uptime parity — adjacent-on-sparse-transient rubble ≈ same-tile-on-dense foliage, and the packages are cousins (theirs 0/1+2, ours 1/0+2). Balance dial if play disagrees = the conditional bonus (+2→+1) at the numbers sitting, NOT the scope. **Rubble-heap inclusion RULED IN, built same exchange: base `Rubble` carries a NEW NARROW tag `Cleo_TerraFirma_IsRubbleCover` via Merges (widening IsAnyStone refused per the trade-goods narrow-tag discipline; scavenger/sweeper/gloves all Takeable-gated so no blast radius); the cloak accepts either tag.** Build note: the `--`-in-XML-comment trap struck an 8th time on this merge AND the invalid file briefly deployed (validation exception didn't halt the copy) — fixed, revalidated with try/catch-exit, redeployed; future validations should fail-closed like that. Gearset draft updated with pointers (bracers + catch gloves rows now live here). Deployed: 3 scripts + Creatures/ObjectBlueprints/PopulationTables. Draft debt: all dice/chances/weights (numbers sitting), three descriptions + creature description (signoff), sprites ×4. **MUTATION NODE BUG — CAUGHT VIA WATCHER BACKLOG + FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-08-14 (strict-valid fail-closed, hash-verified):** the 08-13 build shipped `<mutation Name="Earthen Barrage">` — mutation nodes resolve by CLASS name, so the engine logged `MODERROR ... Could not find XRL.World.Parts.Mutation.Earthen Barrage` + the unnamed-node warn every boot and the baboon spawned WITHOUT EB entirely (no barrage, no whorl, no orbiting pebbles — the whole register). Now `Cleo_TerraFirma_EarthenBarrage` (base `Burrowing` on the urchin only ever worked because display name and class coincide there). ~~`[verify]` next boot: zero Creatures.xml MODERROR at launch~~ **MODERROR-GONE CONFIRMED at the same-evening boot (mod scan clean of the Earthen Barrage line + the unnamed-node warn).** `[verify]` remaining: wished cobble baboon throws stones + whorls at EB 4. **0.1.2 SCOPE RULED 2026-08-14 (user: "cobble baboon shouldn't ship in 0.1.2 btw"): the baboon does NOT debut in the patch.** Current state already satisfies — grep-verified zero table entries/merges, `ExcludeFromDynamicEncounters` on, blueprint ships as an inert wish-door per the spawn-layer gating philosophy — so the ruling's real force is forward-looking: NO spawn wiring (snapjaw-party merges, canyon tables, cobblefur loot surfaces) lands before the stonecarver pass, whatever else 0.1.2 picks up.

Recon (base XML, same day): the snapjaw variant family IS the cheapest creature idiom in the game — Scavenger/Hunter/Shotgunner/Brute/Warrior all inherit `Snapjaw` as thin gear+tile children (base Creatures.xml:2442-2523), pure XML; base `Pickaxe` blueprint exists (`Items.xml:11166`, inherits Tool) for the hand slot. Everything else open, user's: what a miner snapjaw *wants* (scavenging quarry sites? aping the carvers? just digging for grubs?), spawn placement, whether it digs for real (base `Digging`/pickaxe grammar vs flavor). *(Claude float, unruled: snapjaws sniffing around quarried-stone sites would give the block-yard/chalk-border content a natural antagonist — the temptation loop from the other direction, somebody ELSE robbing the carvers.)* **PLACEMENT + TEMPERAMENT LEANS BANKED (user 2026-08-13, "i lean" — soft, confirm at build; formal roster confirm still owed): (1) can spawn in ANY snapjaw group, (2) also sometimes canyon-wild, (3) "likes digging walls as much as attacking."** **Digging recon DONE same day (decomp, Claude): the temperament is 100% stock — zero C#, the pickaxe IS the AI.** Chain: base `Pickaxe` = `MeleeWeapon` (1d3+1, Axe skill) + `Drill` (+20 pen vs walls, wall dies after 8 penetrating hits) + `DiggingTool`; `DiggingTool` installs the `Digging` part on the wielder while properly equipped (EquippedEvent → `RequirePart<Digging>`, removed on unequip via part-support); `Digging` answers `ShouldAttackToReachTargetEvent` → he swings at diggable walls between him and a target; and `Drill` ITSELF answers `PathAsBurrowerEvent` while functional → `GameObject.PathAsBurrower` → nav flag 2 in `Zone.CalculateNav` → the route planner treats walls as passable-by-digging even when just traveling. Precedents: **Worm of the Earth** (no burrow mutation at all — beak carries exactly Drill+DiggingTool, and it honeycombs zones; living proof the item alone drives tunneling) and **mopango pilgrim/tombdweller** (carry `BurrowingClaws PathAsBurrower="false"` — the knob exists because default-on wall-chewing had to be opted OUT of for village NPCs). `[verify]` at build, in-game: idle-wander digging frequency (planner digs when traveling somewhere; how often pure wandering picks wall cells = watch one wished-up miner, Player.log session). Build shape when picked up: party membership = population-table merge into `SnapjawParty0/1/2` (group name confirmed `Creatures`, pickeach — `Load="Merge"` idiom, smoke-check at build); canyon-wild = `DynamicObjectsTable:DesertCanyon_Creatures` tag (jilted-lover idiom). Open at build (user's): tier structure (base idiom = 0/1/2 thin children w/ scaled stats, Scavenger precedent — vs one blueprint in all three parties), party chance number (Claude will propose Hunter-ish 15–25 at build), name lock ("snapjaw miner" presumed), description (user voice per text ownership). **BUILT 2026-08-13: `Cleo_TerraFirma_Snapjaw Miner` (Creatures.xml) as the BEHAVIOR PARENT in the base two-layer idiom — recon upgrade at build time: tier children (Scavenger 0/1/2 precedent) are pure `InventoryPopulationTable` swaps on an untiered parent, so this blueprint forecloses nothing; tier ruling waits at the wiring pass as recorded. Pickaxe direct-inventoried (the AI, per the recon above); HP 6 DRAFT (Hunter-grade over base 3); identity kept NEUTRAL in the description on purpose (what he *wants* is still open). Compiled/deployed with the hound; checklist entry added.**

Verified hook: base `StiltAnimalPen` table (Six Day Stilt) = pickone farmer+flock groups (Farm Pig w5 / Farm Goat w4 / rares w1) — merge a rare-weight group: 2–6 `Farm Boar Vessel` + the base `StiltPigFarmer`. Third thin child of the ruled base blueprint (base → Husangeion pet layer / → Farm pen layer — the inverted hierarchy paying off). Convergence note: joins the ruled Stilt merchant tent = two mod presences at the Stilt. Dynamic-village livestock tables = optional recon at build if wanted beyond the Stilt pen.** **Gut cistern (FLOATED, user, same sitting; confirm owed) — SHAPE CORRECTED by user same sitting: TWO compartments, not Claude's one-tank read: "it collects rainwater in its open back, and something inside it leaches the salt as it moves into a fresh water container."** Recon: rain is LORE-REAL, SIM-ABSENT (base books mention rains; no rain mechanic/liquid exists) — description may honestly say rainwater; mechanical intake must be simulated sources. Build shape (Claude lean): the ruled custom anatomy grows a **gut cistern body slot** + natural-equipment cistern item with its own LiquidVolume (one object can't carry two LiquidVolume parts) — basin = creature's own tank UNSEALED (open back), cistern = sealed internal item, leach = transfer part ticking only the water fraction inward. **Free consequence to protect: basin CONCENTRATES salt as fresh migrates — visible in both readouts, and it sends herds back to pools to re-dilute (writes the grazing behavior).** Death-drop native (equipment falls with contents — the cistern IS the loot, no butchery machinery); pet decant via companion trade. Intake RULED (user, same sitting): "the rain that fills it up doesn't need to be a mechanical thing — it can just be flavor that uses waterproducer" — basin fills via LiquidProducer (the pet's own part, re-aimed), rain stays description-side; no pool-siphon/graze-fill machinery. (Claude's pool-congregation tableau dies with it — noted so it isn't re-floated.) **Unification bonus: retroactively explains the pet's existing LiquidProducer — all boar vessels purify, the Husangeion is a well-tended one. Economy note: upgrades the salty guard from wall to TIMER (fresh-filled = worthless brine, long-grazed = currency) — third creature on the accumulation signature, first where the value is literal money, so purification RATE is the load-bearing number at the numbers sitting (ceiling precedent: pet producer 200–2000 turns into 64 drams). **Rate direction RULED (user, same sitting): "they'd probably fill up pretty slow"** — slow is the shape; exact dice at the numbers sitting per the standing delegation.** Open at pickup: fill mechanism (LiquidProducer re-dial vs time-gated), herd structure, habitat, hostility/passivity (grazing prey lean), cistern confirm + rate. **BUILD ORDER (user 2026-08-11, roster talk): first tinker target — base blueprint first, verify the wild form works, THEN re-parent Husangeion onto it** (the existing-save `[verify]` rides that last step). **BUILD TALK-THROUGH RULINGS (same day, second sitting):** (1) **cistern IN v1** (user: "i would lean implement this on v1 but it depends on tech debt. since we already verified mostly everything works with husangeion, it's cheap to add something here"). **TECH DEBT RESOLVED TO ZERO C# (user asked "is the C# part necessary?"; decomp recon same exchange): the leach = TWO LiquidProducers, one per tank object** — basin (creature's unsealed tank) gets `LiquidProducer Liquid="salt" FillSelfOnly` on a part-water tank (one part does both ruled jobs: fills-with-salty-water AND the concentration consequence; IsNeeded self-terminates at full), cistern item gets its own sealed LiquidVolume + slow `LiquidProducer Liquid="water" FillSelfOnly` (pure fresh inside; producers tick on carried items — proven by our own take-stones Temporary bug). Pet's pure-water producer overrides the base's salt producer by part-name replacement. `LiquidPump` RULED OUT (intake only reads ground puddles, MaxVolume==-1 gate); `ConsumesLiquid` RULED OUT for cross-tank draw (ConsumeLiquid draws from the part's own object, not the wearer). Literal conservation dropped — intake already flavor-ruled, pet precedent for ex-nihilo water. **BUILD CORRECTION: cistern = REAL equipment auto-equipped via inventoryobject into its anatomy slot, NOT DefaultBehavior natural** (naturals don't drop on death; worn equipment drops with contents = the ruled cistern-is-the-loot). Verifies: equipped-item-with-LiquidVolume through save/load (tfserial-style probe) + long-graze eyeball of the basin's salt fraction climbing. **Cistern accessibility (user question, answered from decomp same sitting): NOT player-reachable while equipped — inventory-action menus never open on another creature's worn items; it drops SEALED on death (the loot beat). The BASIN (creature's own tank) IS reachable on a living adjacent vessel per the established pet behavior — wild = unsealed brackish, correctly worthless.
